# Decision Register — please fill in

How to use: for each item tick an option (`[x]`) or write under **Answer**. If you don't know yet, tick **"Use the proposed default for now"** — the plan proceeds on that default and the gate column says when a real answer is needed. Leave **Confirmed by / date** for the person who owns the decision. I will not start Phase 1+ design work that depends on an item until it has either an answer or an explicit "use default".

Legend — *Gate*: the sub-phase where the decision becomes irreversible without rework.

### HDPE validation dataset — owner clarification, 15 October 2026

The owner authorized cleanup of the local demonstration data, realistic HDPE
seeding and full application/persona/UI testing. The final retained topology is
one organization named **HDPE**, its **HDPE** plant, and **unit100 / Extruder1**
plus **unit200 / Extruder2**; remove the other organizations and their child data.
The schema's organization → plant → unit levels remain intact. Temporary extra
plants are permitted only as test fixtures and are removed before handover.

Engineering values are synthetic examples because no actual plant dataset or
historian credentials were supplied. See `docs/HDPE_DEMO_GUIDE.md` for the declared
assumptions and `plans/HDPE_E2E_VALIDATION.md` for execution/evidence. The owner also
explicitly authorized sub-agents and browser testing during this validation run.

### D02 — PI Web API installation *(Owner: PI administrator · Gate: 1.6 certification)*
Please provide or confirm:
1. PI Web API version and base URL; authentication mode (Basic / Kerberos / Bearer); who provides credentials; is a service account available?
2. AF database and element scope(s) to monitor.
3. **Event frame templates** actually used and their attribute names. Our defaults: `Production Loss` (Description, ReasonCode, OEECode, ImpactCode, Quantity, Cost, Shift, Crew, Duration, LossCategory, OperatorName), `IOW Deviation`, `SOL Violation`, `APM Alert`, `Failure WO`, `Production Output` (GoodQty, ScrapQty).
4. How an **open (still running) event frame** is represented — we assume `EndTime = 9999-12-31T23:59:59Z`.
5. Are reason/state values stored as **enumeration sets (digital states)**?
6. Which **tags** give production actuals: output event frames, totalizers (counters), or rate tags? Units and timebase (e.g. t/h).
7. Does the installation offer a change feed / archive corrections? Are deletions of event frames possible?
8. Polling cadence allowed (default 60 s) and historical backfill depth you want (default 90 days).
- **Answer:**
we already have web api - also sdk 
- Confirmed by / date:

### D03 — Calendars, shifts, crews, timezone *(Owner: operations · Gate: 2.1)*
Proposed default: one site per tenant; IANA timezone from the plant setting; operational day starts **06:00** local; **3 × 8 h shifts** (06–14, 14–22, 22–06); **shift is the accounting leaf**, day and month are roll-ups; crews are a separate rotation.

2. Operational day start: ______
3. Shift pattern (names, start/end, days): ______
4. Accounting leaf: [ ] shift [ ] day only
5. Holidays / planned shutdown handling: [ ] excluded from scheduled time [ ] counted as planned downtime inside schedule
6. Crews: [ ] fixed per shift [ ] rotating pattern (describe) [ ] not tracked
- [ ] Use the proposed default for now

### D04 — Baselines and OEE policy *(Owner: production owner · Gate: 2.5 / 2.7 fixture sign-off)*
Proposed default: compute **both** ledgers; the **plan ledger** is the default view; **planned stops inside scheduled time reduce availability**; schedule exclusions only via the calendar; derates count as **performance** loss; micro-stops < 60 s count as performance, ≥ 60 s as availability.
1. Default view: [ ] plan ledger [ ] capacity ledger [ ] both side by side
2. Planned maintenance inside the schedule: [ ] reduces availability (default; **SME 2026-10-13 Q6: correct for stops inside planned production time — the earlier "ISO 22400 default" label was imprecise; only time outside the production schedule is excluded, via calendar exclusions**) [ ] excluded from scheduled time
3. Derate (reduced rate) counts as: [ ] performance [ ] availability (fractional)
4. Micro-stop threshold: ______ seconds (default 60)
5. Quantity basis for time losses: [ ] sustained capacity rate for capacity ledger and planning rate for plan ledger (default) [ ] other
6. Do you also want the **GE-style reconciled OEE** tile? [ ] yes [ ] no
- [ ] Use the proposed default for now

as per standard

### D13 — Failure taxonomy, repair evidence, RCA thresholds *(Owner: reliability lead · Gate: 2.7 / 3.9)*

| 2026-10-08 | 1.2 Source vs user layers | A source re-sync writes only fields whose **source** value changed and that the user has not overridden; overridden fields open a `source_vs_override_conflict` review item and keep the user value. Overrides cover source-owned fields plus the classification axes; reason/impact/OEE codes and workflow states are the user's layer and are never touched by a source (PI-mapped codes are taken at create only) | engineering default under ING06 / §5.3, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.2 Duplicates / retraction | Identical source content is a duplicate (counter only, no revision, ETag unchanged); retraction tombstones (`retracted`, legacy "Cancelled"), never deletes | engineering default under ING01 |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.3 Mapping precedence | Exact source id → exact normalised path → pattern (higher `precedence` first) → approved AF binding (element, then ancestors); a connection-specific rule beats an "any connection" rule; two hits of one tier with different targets are **ambiguous and quarantined**, never resolved by picking one. Paths and patterns: `\\` and `/` are the same separator, case-insensitive; `*` spans separators, `?` one character, `[*]` `[?]` `[[]` literals | engineering default under ING03, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.3 Unresolved codes | A delivered reason/impact/OEE value that matches no code (or two codes) exactly leaves the event **uncoded** with a diagnostic and suggestions; the event itself is not quarantined | engineering default under ING03 (spec says quarantine), not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.3 Durations and attributes | PI `Duration` defaults to minutes (as 1.1); TimeSpan text is parsed only under a `timespan` rule; when a supplied duration and the timestamps differ by more than 1 s the timestamps win and `DURATION_MISMATCH` is recorded | engineering default under ING04, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.3 Code libraries | Codes are unique per tenant and library; a change is a new version (history kept), retire ends validity and never deletes, a parent with active children cannot be retired; failure groups follow class → problem → cause → remedy; shared tenant-less codes are no longer accepted on events (left in the DB) | engineering default under §3.2 / NFR01, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.3 Code-driven classification | A reason code proposes schedule class, OEE component and impact code (rule in force at the event start, else the code's defaults); a value the user sends wins and is flagged `overridesDefault` | engineering default under §3.2, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.3 Write roles | Code libraries and code rules: `master_data_admin` or `manager_approver` (TenantAdmin/Manager); source mappings and attribute rules: `integration_admin` or `manager_approver`; reads: any tenant member | engineering default under D06, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.4 Split / merge | Split at an instant strictly inside the event (before now when open) → two children sharing the parent's occurrence and coding; merge only within one unit, same physical type and schedule class unless `overrideClassification`, gap policy default 0 s (≤ 1 day), conflicting non-null coding → 422; contributors and split parents become `superseded` (v1 "Cancelled", not editable), nothing is deleted | engineering default under DT09, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.4 Amounts on split/merge | `quantity` and `cost` are prorated by duration on split (an open parent's amounts go to the running child) and summed on merge, so totals are conserved | engineering default, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.4 Bulk coding | Codes = reason, schedule class, OEE component (reason defaults apply); ≤ 500 events atomic and refused whole on any exclusion; > 500 (max 10 000) → job in chunks of 200, each chunk atomic, job `partial` when some chunks fail | engineering default under DT10 / API04, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.4 Close | End after start and not in the future; on a source-owned event the end is a user override with a `pending_source_confirmation` review item, resolved automatically when the source delivers the same end; a different source end opens a conflict and the override stays | engineering default under DT10 / DT11, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.4 Operation rights | Split, merge, close and bulk coding need tenant (and unit-scope) membership only, as v2 PATCH; role gating comes with the 3.2 UI | engineering default, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.5 Ordering and duplicates | A repeated message id is a duplicate (counted, no new raw row); same content under a new id is `duplicate_content`; a later source observation time (tie: higher numeric source revision) is a new revision; an earlier one is `stale` (kept raw, not applied); an unprovable tie with different content is an `order_conflict` exception — never an overwrite | engineering default under §5.1 / ING01 / FND04, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.5 PI delivery identity | PI frames have no version: message id = WebId + content hash, observation time = read time; an unchanged re-read is a duplicate; a redelivery of a quarantined frame retries its normalisation (exception occurrences = retries) | engineering default under ING01 / ING05, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.5 Quarantine and replay | Unplaceable/ambiguous records go to `ingestion_exceptions` (PI frames are also mirrored into v1 `unmapped_events`); resolution = fix the mapping and replay (re-delivers the latest retained version with its original identity), or close as irrecoverable (kept); a successful normalisation resolves the object's open exceptions | engineering default under ING05, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.5 Leases, retries, outbox | Job lease 60 s with heartbeat every 50 records; a failed page retries with backoff 5 s × 2^n (cap 15 min) and the job becomes `failed` after 10 retries; bulk-job lease 120 s; outbox retries 2 s × 2^n (cap 10 min), dead letter (`failed`) after 12 attempts; all tunable by env | engineering default under ING02 / FND02 / NFR07, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.5 Raw retention | Raw observations are immutable, partitioned monthly by intake time, payloads inline; no retention/purge yet (decide with ops sizing, D07) | engineering default under FND01, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.5 Intake roles | Connections and ingestion jobs (create, test, discover, start/pause/resume/drain/stop) and replay / exception resolution need `integration_admin` or `manager_approver`; reads: any tenant member; secrets are write-only | engineering default under §13.2 / D06, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.6 PI polling and recovery | Window poll 60 s with a 15 min overlap (first window: 60 min lookback), pages of 500, every page read; open-frame refresh 60 s; correction rescans over 7 days (daily) and 90 days (weekly); inventory comparison over 7 days (daily); attribute reads in batch chunks of 250; v1 job interval kept when set | engineering default under PI04 / PI05, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.6 PI HTTP and failures | 30 s timeout, 3 attempts with full-jitter backoff (500 ms base, cap 10 s), `Retry-After` honoured up to 60 s, no redirects, 64 MiB response cap (browser route 5 MiB); TLS always verified unless the connection sets `allowInsecureTls`; 401/403/400/404 fail the job at once with health `auth_failed`/`authz_failed`/… and no cursor move | engineering default under PI01 / NFR07 / §4.2, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.6 Open frames and deletions | End time ≥ 9999-01-01 = still open (end null, raw sentinel kept); a deletion counts only after a complete inventory listing **and** a 404 on direct lookup, and is a tombstone (`deleted_confirmed`, event retracted, never deleted); restatement proposals for approved periods come with 2.8 | engineering default under PI03 / PI06 / D02 items 4 and 7, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-08 | 1.6 v1 PI jobs and browser route | v1 sync jobs are linked to leased ingestion jobs (window from the v1 watermark + open-frame, rescan and inventory jobs) and show their status; the v1 on-demand pull reads every page (`maxCount` = page size) and moves the watermark only after an error-free pull; `pi-browser` requests: allowlisted controllers, GET or POST `/batch` only, caller's own tenant | engineering default under PI04 / §4.2 / I8 / I12, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 1.7 Measurement identity and corrections | A PI sample is source object `<WebId>@<timestamp>`; an unchanged re-read is a duplicate; a changed value at the same timestamp read later is a new revision (read time orders it) and the latest revision is used; samples are never updated or deleted | engineering default under §5.1 / PI07 / PI09, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 1.7 Counter rules | Drops are never production: `known_instant` resets add nothing between the bracketing readings (≤ max gap); `detect_drop` + modulus adds `M − a + b` when the implied rate ≤ `expected_max` per `rate_timebase` (accepted when no `expected_max` is set); anything else is `reset_unresolved` with a null quantity; a step longer than `max_gap_seconds` is missing coverage and its delta is not counted | engineering default under ACT02 / ACT03 / T26 / T27, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 1.7 Rate rules | Step or linear per signal, `value × Δt / timebase`; bad (and, unless accepted, uncertain) samples and values outside `[expected_min (default 0), expected_max]` are excluded and reported; no rate without a configured timebase; PI `Total` only for rate signals whose job confirms the timebase PI assumes for it | engineering default under ACT04 / PI09 / T25, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 1.7 Signal defaults and polling | `max_gap_seconds` 900, interpolation step, reset policy none; recorded poll lookback 60 min, overlap 15 min, 500 values per stream per page; summaries hourly; lost-marker backfill ≤ 24 h; preview ≤ 31 days / 50 000 samples; user backfill ≤ 90 days ending by now; ≤ 200 signals per job; units compared case- and space-insensitively, never converted | engineering default under PI07 / PI08 / D02 item 8 / D07, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 1.7 Historian-tag crosswalk | Only enabled tags with a WebId, in a tenant with exactly one active PI connection, whose AF element maps to exactly one unit by `exact_path` are seeded — as `other` signals (no quantity until an engineer sets type, timebase and reset policy) | engineering default under §14.2 / MIG §18.2, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.1 DST and boundaries | A shift is every instant whose plant-local wall time lies in its `[start, end)`: a spring-forward gap contributes no time (a boundary in the gap moves to its end), the fall-back hour counts twice; a boundary inside the repeated hour gives two instances with different start offsets, a shift containing the whole repeated hour stays one instance with start/end offsets differing; days are 23/25 h, never 24 h forced; offsets stored in minutes, local labels stored | engineering default under CAL02 / §3.3, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.1 Operational day and versions | A shift starting before the operational day start (06:00, D03) belongs to the previous operational date (production date); `daysOfWeek` and holidays refer to operational dates; effective dates are operational dates (`effective_to` exclusive); the highest published version in force wins, a unit override before its site calendar; published versions are immutable (new changes = new version, DB trigger) | engineering default under CAL01, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.1 Exclusions and holidays | Holidays `excluded` take all shifts of that operational date out of scheduled time (D03); `reduced` keeps them and only warns (no reduction rule yet); exclusions are instants with an offset and split the instances they cut (`exclusion_kind` planned_shutdown / turnaround / no_demand); with working intervals defined, shift time outside them is `non_working`; excluded time is the capacity ledger's `schedule_exclusion` bucket | engineering default under CAL01 / ACC01, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.1 Materialization | Instances kept generated 90 operational days ahead (hourly worker top-up, gap-fill only); a publication generates from max(effectiveFrom, today − 366 d), regenerates future dates (from tomorrow) and only fills missing past dates; re-publishing needs a reason and may regenerate past dates from `regenerateFrom` (audited); regeneration compares content hashes per date and writes nothing when equal | engineering default under CAL01 / plan 2.1 step 4, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.1 Sites, crews, legacy label, roles | One default site per tenant from `tenants.timezone` (UTC with `timezone_fallback` when it is not an IANA name), created by the migration or on first use; no calendar is seeded (the D03 calendar is created through the API / 3.4 UI). Crew rotations: cycle of shift names from an anchor date, one crew per instance (two → unassigned + warning), never alter bounds or scheduled time. v1 `shift` is read from the instance containing the event start, else the stored text. Calendar, crew and rotation writes: `master_data_admin` or `manager_approver`; reads and previews: any tenant member | engineering default under D03 / §13.2, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.2 Versions and approval | Profiles, rates and valuation rates are effective-dated `[from, to)` versions numbered per family (unit/product/mode; profile + kind; owner + basis); drafts are edited, approved rows never are — they are only closed by a supersede or retired. Approving an overlapping version is 409 `VERSION_OVERLAP` naming the conflicts; `supersede` closes the one predecessor that starts earlier at exactly the new `from` (never shortens a version beyond what the new one replaces); drafts may overlap. No submitter ≠ approver rule for master data (D06 applies to accounting revisions) | engineering default under PLAN01 / D06, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.2 Rates | Zero is refused for nameplate / demonstrated / sustained / planning (they divide quantities) and allowed for budget / forecast; a rate starts inside its profile version; resolution picks the approved profile and the approved rate per kind in force at the instant, none → `no_profile`; rates are per the profile `quantity_uom`, units never converted | engineering default under PLAN01 / KPI01, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.2 Legacy bridge | One **draft** profile per same-tenant (unit, product) of v1 plans, from the first plan start; v1 `capacity` (no timebase), `ideal_cycle_time` and `products.standard_rate` kept as review values only — no sustained or planning rate is created, `standard_rate` shown as "unconfirmed legacy cost" until D08 Q3 is answered; plans need explicit rates (no 100/h default) | engineering default under MIG §18.2 / D05 / D08, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.2 Imports | Budget / forecast / MPC documents (JSON or CSV, ≤ 5 000 rows) are applied whole or not at all by the worker with a per-line report; rows need an approved profile at their `from` and must not overlap each other or approved rates of another document; a later delivery of the same external id is a correction (earlier rows retired, new rows approved, `source = import`); MPC = sustained rate; importing needs `manager_approver` because it writes approved rows | engineering default under PLAN06, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.2 Valuation rates and roles | Contribution margin needs a profile, cost per hour an impact code, standard cost either; `per_uom` = the profile quantity unit or `h`; currency ISO 4217 per row (D08 Q2 still open). Drafts of profiles / rates / valuation rates: `master_data_admin` or `manager_approver`; approve, retire, import: `manager_approver`; reads: any tenant member (unit-scoped callers see their units) | engineering default under VAL01 / D08 / §13.2, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.3 Versions and scenarios | A plan header owns versions; each version keeps its own scenario, unit and window `[valid_from, valid_to)` (whole UTC seconds, ≤ 400 days); at most one open (draft/submitted) and one approved version per plan; a change after approval is a new draft (n + 1). Every tenant has a default `Baseline` scenario (migration or first use); exactly one scenario per tenant is the accounting baseline; creating a scenario with `isAccountingBaseline` moves the flag | engineering default under PLAN02 / PLAN04, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.3 Target distribution | Leaves = stored shift instances clipped to the plan window (excluded instances → 0 s); profile and rates in force at the leaf start apply to the whole leaf (a boundary inside is a warning); quantity targets are split by planning capacity (default), slice weights then capacity, or equally over eligible leaves; each share truncated to 8 places, the remainder on the last eligible leaf, so Σ = target exactly; time basis = planning capacity per leaf; manual targets must add up to the header; a positive target without eligible time is refused | engineering default under PLAN02 / PLAN03, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.3 Campaigns and concurrent products | Sequential campaigns take the leaves that start inside them and must not overlap; `independent` / `shared` streams only with `CONCURRENT_PRODUCTS=true` (one stream kind per version). Joint recipe = `productMixConstraints.shares[productId]` on each shared slice's `shared_capacity` profile: every share > 0 and Σ ≤ 1 per leaf, shared time split by share; quantity units are never added (totals per unit; a header target over different units is refused) | engineering default under D14 / PLAN05 / T43, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.3 Approval and restatement | Approval needs generated periods and a manager approver; approved slices of one scenario, unit and product never overlap (409 `PLAN_OVERLAP`, DB exclusion); the previous approved version becomes `superseded`, its periods stay frozen (trigger); the restatement proposal is deferred to 2.4/2.8 via the `PlanVersionApproved` outbox message (`restatementRequired`); no submitter ≠ approver rule for plans (D06 governs accounting revisions) | engineering default under PLAN04 / D06, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.3 Legacy bridge and roles | v1 plan writes are limited to the caller's tenant / unit scope; a v1 create or edit of unit, product, dates or `plannedQuantity` updates the open draft (or opens n + 1 after approval) and regenerates its periods synchronously (refusals stored on the draft, the v1 save succeeds); v1 "Approved" goes through the approval service, "Pending Review/Approval" → submitted, "Draft/Rejected" → draft; client approval names/times are ignored; approved plans are not deleted. Plans and generation: `production_engineer` or `manager_approver`; approval and scenarios: `manager_approver` (scenarios tenant-wide); persisted generation is a 202 job that keeps the requester's unit scope | engineering default under C4 / §13.2 / API04, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.4 Periods and slices | One period per stored shift instance (bounds, operational date, shift and exclusion copied; the link drops when the calendar regenerates) for units with an approved profile; periods never overlap; an instance overlapping a period with other bounds is skipped, not forced. Slices come from approved plan periods of the accounting-baseline scenario, else from the unit's single approved profile; several or no default candidates leave the period without slices and say why. The worker creates periods for instances started in the last 48 h; backfill ≤ 92 days | engineering default under §3.1 / D03, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.4 Authority and output events | The versioned `source_authority` policy (D05 default: Production Output EFs primary, PI counter fallback on missing / stale / bad quality) selects one source per slice and period until 1.10; both quantified and differing beyond `conflictTolerance` (0) → `source_conflict` and quality `conflict`, the primary is kept, never summed; several unflagged signals of one source → `ambiguous_signal`. An output event's `quantity` is gross, `defective_quantity` its rejects; open events are not booked; a spanning event is split in proportion to time and marked estimated (`allowProportionalEstimate`, D05 seed) or, when forbidden, booked whole at its end with `spanning_event` in every period it touches; output-event coverage = union of booked event intervals | engineering default under D05 / ACT02 / SRC01 (1.10 pending), not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.4 Actuals results | good = gross − reject + accepted rework; rejects unknown → reject null and good = gross (`rejects_not_measured`, no quality factor); only good known → gross null; missing data is null, never zero; units never converted (`uom_mismatch`); quality complete only with a quantity, coverage 1 and no problem finding; a new immutable run is written only when the input hash (slices, events, samples, adjustments, report lines, policy and engine versions) changes, so loss-explanation edits reuse the result (T44); readiness `complete_inputs` once the period ended and every slice is complete with the required coverage | engineering default under ACT01 / ACT05 / ACT07 / T44, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.4 Adjustments | Manual actuals are proposed on an active slice with reason, evidence, signed non-zero quantity in the slice unit, basis measured / estimated and an optional window inside the slice; every approval or rejection is by someone other than the author (also when no approved report is affected); rejection needs a reason; approved adjustments are a separate component (method `mixed` / `manual`) and their windows count toward coverage under `countApprovedSubstitutionsAsAccepted`; they follow the product's new slice when slices are re-resolved. Propose / recompute / materialize: `production_engineer` or `manager_approver`; approve / reject: `manager_approver` | engineering default under ACT06 / D06 / §13.2, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.4 External daily-production records | Certified-eligible statuses `COMPLETED`, `RELEASED` (env `EXTERNAL_REPORT_CERTIFIED_STATUSES`; D05 Q4 still open); same id + revision with the same content is a duplicate, other content 409; a numerically higher (else later-imported) revision supersedes the current one even when not certified, a late lower one is stored superseded; a record is the authority only when the policy says `external_report` and only for a period with exactly its UTC bounds (daily leaf), never pro-rated over shifts; import (JSON or CSV job) needs a tenant-wide grant and `manager_approver` or `integration_admin` | engineering default under ACT08 / §3.1 / D05, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.4 Recalculation and policy store | Recalculation requests (`PeriodRecalculateRequested`) come from output-event changes, adjustment approvals, report imports and baseline plan approvals (slices of reconciled periods are never re-resolved; restatement proposals are 2.8); a settled sweep re-queues periods ≤ 7 days old that ended ≥ 900 s ago without a run or with samples stored after their run; jobs keep the requester's unit scope. `accounting_policies` holds versioned `coverage_gate` / `source_authority` bodies (unit version before tenant version), seeded with the D05 defaults per tenant; no policy API yet | engineering default under FND02 / FND03 / API04 / 0.2, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.5 Ledgers and residuals | Both ledgers per slice and run: capacity at the sustained rate per atomic segment (cut at schedule / exclusion, stop, micro-stop, derate and rate-version edges), exclusion and availability by segment, performance = max(0, running capacity − gross) and quality = gross − good computed once and allocated to causes (micro-stops < 60 s and fractional derates at the ledger rate, measured slowdowns and quality events by quantity, prorated into the slice), remainder without cause, `gain` above reference, signed residual never clamped; only good known → residual = running capacity − good; good unknown or unit mismatch → residual null; claims beyond the component fail the run (`IMPOSSIBLE_CLAIM`), as do good > gross and negative values | engineering default under ACC01–ACC03 / D04, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.5 Plan ledger | Target = plan-period target, actual = good; automatic candidates (availability and positive performance at the time-phased planning rate, quality declared / measured) are active lines until 2.8 overrides; no planning rate over the scheduled time → no automatic lines, residual = gap, `plan_allocation_required`; candidates beyond the gap kept and flagged `over_accounted` + `bridge_required` (never scaled); overproduction with attributed loss = `conflict` and not over-accounted; tolerance = policy epsilon (D06 zero, 1e-6) | engineering default under ACC01 / ACC04 / D06, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.5 Cause allocation | Until 1.10: one cause per stop occurrence per segment (its earliest active event), overlapping occurrences share the segment equally, shares truncated to 8 places with the remainder on the last occurrence id (Σ = 1 exactly); split / merge children keep the occurrence so allocation is unchanged; planned stops inside the schedule reduce availability (D04), excluded only via the calendar | engineering default under ACC05 / ACC06 / D04, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.5 Runs and reproducibility | A run is serialized per period (advisory lock + period row lock), runs actuals first, freezes its inputs (event revisions, occurrences, actuals run, plan version / periods, rate versions, calendar versions, slices, policy versions, engine version) into a fingerprint and sha256 hash; equal to the current or latest failed run → no run; else `running` → `validated` (lines, evidence, totals; old run `superseded`, draft pointer switched in the same transaction) or `failed` (errors kept, pointer unchanged). Open events count to now while the period runs (then the hash includes it), else to the period end. Only draft revisions are recalculated; in-review / approved revisions go to a 2.8 hook. Loss-event changes (downtime, slowdown, quality) request recalculation; profile / rate approvals do not yet (next recompute or sweep) | engineering default under FND03 / ACC08, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.5 Policies, routes, roles | `oee_policy` (D04) and `reconciliation_tolerance` (D06) join `accounting_policies`, seeded per tenant; reads `GET /production-periods/{id}/accounting` (default: approved revision else latest) and `/runs` for any tenant member in scope; `POST /production-periods/recalculate` (≤ 92 days) for `production_engineer` / `manager_approver`, body units of the tenant and scope (422), all units only with a tenant-wide grant (403), jobs keep the requester's scope; v1 `oee-summaries` writes send `Deprecation: true` (410 with 3.5) | engineering default under §13.2 / API04 / §14.3, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.8 Reconciliation lines | A draft save replaces the full set of decisions and manual lines as a new immutable line set (calculation-run lines stay untouched, no new run); decisions name a candidate by its deterministic key with a reason (accept needs none) and keep the quantity seen, so a later run keeps them and flags `baseline_changed` / `candidate_missing` until re-saved; undecided automatic candidates count as attributed (shown `proposed`); manual lines need an event overlapping the slice, an approved adjustment of the period, or an explicit cause without event that is coded or explicitly uncoded; negative quantities only for bridge / gain; a save may not add loss beyond gap + tolerance (bridge / gain and reductions are always allowed) | engineering default under ACC07 / ACC08 / §15.3, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.8 Tolerance and submission gates | Tolerance zero → 0, absolute → value, percent → value % of the target and 0 for a zero target (epsilon is precision only); on submit each slice with epsilon < \|residual\| ≤ tolerance gets one labelled `tolerance_adjustment` line of exactly the signed residual (none for overproducing slices), the residual before adjustment stays visible; submission is refused (422) for residual beyond tolerance, over-accounting beyond tolerance, attributed loss on an overproducing slice, actuals below the coverage gate or in source conflict, a queued / running recalculation, open review items on evidence events, decisions on changed candidates, uncoded lines (no allowance in D06) and Unaccounted lines (D06: not allowed) | engineering default under ACC09 / ACC10 / D06, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.8 Lifecycle and separation of duties | One open (draft or in review) revision per period; submit needs the run and content hash the submitter read, the submitted hash is the sha256 of the reviewed content; approve and reject need a manager approver who is not the submitter — for every role, SuperAdmin and users holding both roles included (403) — and approve needs a non-stale submission whose content still has the submitted hash (409); approval stores a snapshot, labels the period reconciled and is never updated or deleted (trigger), so earlier approved revisions stay as history and the latest approved one is published; reject keeps the reason and returns to draft; revise (manager approver) opens n + 1 over the published revision with its decisions and queues a recalculation; cancel ends drafts only; one approver (no second-approver step) | engineering default under WF01 / WF02 / WF03 / D06, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.8 Late corrections and proposals | A recalculation with a new input hash recalculates a draft (decisions kept), marks an in-review submission stale (`recompute` returns it to draft), and opens or refreshes the one open change proposal of an approved revision with the approved figures and a preview (approved decisions re-applied to the new candidates); accepting a proposal is a revise (plan restatements re-resolve the slices first), dismissing needs a reason; a baseline plan approval with `restatementRequired` opens `plan_change` proposals for reconciled periods | engineering default under WF04 / PLAN04 / §5.3, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.8 Roles, routes and audit | Draft lines, submit, cancel, recompute: `production_engineer` or `manager_approver` in the period's unit scope; approve, reject, revise, proposal decisions: `manager_approver`; reads: any tenant member in scope; other units' periods and proposals are 404, foreign body ids 422 `INVALID_REFERENCE`, jobs keep the requester's scope; every command writes `approval_actions` and the append-only `audit_log` (who, when, from, to, reason, hashes, correlation id); v1 `reconcile-forms` / `loss-categories` writes send `Deprecation: true` (410 with 3.5) | engineering default under §13.2 / WF05 / D06, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.9 Export content and reproducibility | Exports contain approved content only: `published` (latest approved revision per period overlapping the window) or named approved revisions (`REVISION_NOT_APPROVED` otherwise), no provisional / draft export; the revisions, approval snapshots and unit / product labels are pinned when the export is requested and the file is rendered only from them, with the request time in the header, so re-rendering reproduces the sha256; every row names period, revision, approval snapshot and calculation run; reports `period_summary` (per slice) and `loss_lines` (candidates with decision + manual / replace / tolerance lines); decimals as strings; value and currency empty, `valuation_status unvalued` with the D08 reason; CSV (RFC 4180, UTF-8 BOM, CRLF) with text starting `= + - @ TAB CR` prefixed by `'` (plain decimals untouched); PDF deferred to Phase 3 (needs a renderer dependency) | engineering default under AN03 / NFR02 / D08 / WF03, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.9 Export jobs, storage and limits | `POST /exports` → 202 + export job; the worker renders under a lease (300 s, 3 attempts) page by page (200 revisions), checkpointing progress and cancellation; cancel: queued → `cancelled` at once, running → stops at the next page (`partial` when sources were rendered), no file kept, download 410; a completed export is immutable (trigger) and expires after 168 h (file purged hourly, 410 `EXPORT_EXPIRED`); files in a private `EXPORT_STORAGE_DIR` (Docker volume `exports`), never under the public `uploads`, keys `<tenant>/<job>.csv` from uuids (no traversal); at most 50 000 period revisions and 400 days per request; each job family keeps its own table and status route (no generic `jobs` table yet); all tunable by env | engineering default under API04 / NFR02 / NFR07, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.9 Export authorization | Any tenant member may export what it can read; a unit-limited caller names units of its scope (none → 403 `TENANT_SCOPE_REQUIRED`; another unit, another tenant's unit or revision → 422 `INVALID_REFERENCE`); a job's content scope is its unit list (null = tenant-wide) and only callers whose grants cover all of it see its status, sources, cancel and download (404 otherwise), re-checked at download time; requests, cancellations and downloads are written to `audit_log` | engineering default under WF05 / §13.2, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.9 Domain messages and delivery | Workflow messages carry the §15.4 v1 envelope (`schemaVersion, domainEventId, tenantId, entityType, entityId, entityRevision, occurredAt, correlationId`); `PeriodSubmitted`, `PeriodApproved` and `PeriodRestated` (approval of a revision superseding a published one) have JSON Schema contracts in `contracts/domain-events/`; `PeriodApproved` carries per-product approved totals with uom, actuals quality, policy versions, snapshot hash, prior revision and an explicit unvalued valuation; the relay never publishes a payload that violates its contract (dead letter `SCHEMA_INVALID` at once); delivery stays on the outbox with its retries / dead letter (D10, D12: no webhook, CMMS or Kafka republish in R1) and `GET /deliveries/reconciliation` shows each approved revision's delivery; change proposals list the completed exports of the approved revision; realtime sequences come with the 3.1 gateway | engineering default under API05 / WF03 / D10 / D12, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.7 KPI storage and runs | Every KPI is computed inside the 2.5 calculation run from its frozen inputs and ledger totals and stored immutably in `kpi_results` with explicit numerator, denominator, value (null with a reason when not computable — never 0, 87 % or 100 %), quality (`ok` / `provisional` / `unavailable` / `exception` for factors above 100 %), cohort and the `oee_policy` version; scopes: period (unit), product slice, asset, downtime-breakdown cell; reports read and sum stored terms only (no computation or write on read); run engine version `2.5.0+kpi.2.7.0` in the input hash, so drafts get KPIs on their next recompute while approved revisions from before 2.7 stay without them until revised | engineering default under KPI01–KPI03 / AN03 / §14.2, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.7 OEE time model | ISO 22400 per D04: S = scheduled time after calendar exclusions (planned stops inside S per `oee_policy`), D = union of availability stops (≥ micro-stop threshold) inside S, R = S − D; U / V = gross / good at the time-weighted sustained rate of the slice's running time (P = gross / running capacity, the capacity-ledger basis), summed over sequential product slices; concurrent streams per product only (period P / Q / OEE null `concurrent_streams`); only good known → Q and P null `quality_evidence_missing`, OEE from good; actuals not `complete` → provisional; running periods use the whole window like the ledgers; `unknown_time` = stop time with schedule class unknown, `maxUnknownShare` waits for the 1.10 unknown state; reconciled PLA OEE (labelled) from the capacity-ledger G / LA / LP / LQ only when the capacity residual is within epsilon | engineering default under KPI01–KPI04 / D04, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.7 Downtime and reliability | Downtime over the period (calendar) window; overlapping occurrences share each piece equally so every breakdown (reason, impact, OEE component, asset, schedule class, stop kind) adds up to the union; stop count by occurrence start (splits once), carry-in separately, mean stop duration over completed stops ending in the window (open censored); scheduled availability over scheduled time, onstream factor over calendar time. Failure (D13) = `FailureWO` or unplanned stop with a failure code, at least the micro-stop threshold long, a work order overlapping a coded stop of the same asset counted once; MTBF = unit run time / failures starting in the window (null `no_failures` keeping exposure and count; an asset's exposure = its unit's run time); no repair-evidence source → MTTR null `repair_evidence_missing`, `mean downtime to restore` reported and labelled | engineering default under KPI05–KPI08 / D13, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.7 KPI reports and scope | `GET /v2/reports/oee`, `/downtime`, `/reliability`: periods by start in [from, to) ≤ 400 days, shown run = latest approved revision else the open one (`approvedOnly` = approved only), sums of stored terms in SQL, site-local keys from the stored operational date / shift, `calculationRunIds[]`, provisional when a draft or a provisional / missing row contributes, coverage = share of periods with a shown run; reads for any tenant member; named units / assets must be of the tenant and the caller's scope (422 `INVALID_REFERENCE`); a unit-limited caller naming no unit is restricted to its own units (not refused); v1 `/reports/oee-summary` and `/oee-trends` (mean of percentages) send `Deprecation: true` until 3.6 | engineering default under AN03 / API03 / WF05, not yet reviewed by the owner |

Replaces "single currency per tenant / no FX" | owner: "for currency we should have both irr & $" |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.6 Valuation policy and approval | `valuation_basis` is an `accounting_policies` kind: existing tenants keep v1 (contracts default, currency `XXX`, history, never approvable) and get v2 (IRR and USD, `fxPolicy` snapshot, contribution margin default, `exclusive`), both **unapproved**; new tenants get one unapproved IRR / USD version on first use. Nothing values before a manager approver with a tenant-wide grant approves a version (VAL01 sign-off; author ≠ approver); valuation applies the highest approved version in force, else records the unapproved one with every line `unvalued` / `policy_unapproved`. Approvals of policies, valuation rates or FX rates do not queue recalculations (next recompute, recalculate route or sweep) | engineering default under VAL01 / D08 / 0.2, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.6 Valuation in the run | Valuation runs inside the 2.5 calculation run from a frozen context (policy body, approved rate and FX versions with their values) stored on the run and hashed, so a later approval revalues drafts, marks in-review revisions stale and opens change proposals for approved ones; engine version `+val.2.6.0`. Views: contribution margin and standard cost per quantity of the slice's profile (rate unit must equal the line unit, never converted), cost per hour per lost second of availability lines whose cause has an impact code (interval × share); time-uniform lines split at rate-version edges, other lines take the rate at the segment start; a gap in the rate history leaves the line unvalued; views are never added except the separately named `independent_components` view for impact codes the policy lists; kinds never netted (gains, residuals in their own rows); stored with 8 dp, canonical decimal strings in the API | engineering default under VAL01–VAL03 / FND03 / D08, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-09 | 2.6 Currencies and FX | Every valued line is reported in each policy currency (IRR, USD); valuation rates must be entered in one of them (422 `CURRENCY_NOT_REPORTED`). FX rates are versioned per tenant and currency pair (`1 from = rate to`, > 0, ≤ 10 dp, `[from, to)`, draft → approved → retired, no overlap of approved versions, supersede at the boundary); conversion uses the approved version in force at each component's valuation date, the inverse pair divides; no covering rate → amount null, reason `fx_rate_missing`, never estimated. Currency totals are null when any contributing valued line lacks a conversion (`nativeAmount` keeps the per-currency sum); missing prices keep their quantity (`unvaluedQuantity`, status `mixed`) | engineering default under D08 Q2 / VAL03, not yet reviewed by the owner |

Exports: `valuation_view, value_1, currency_1, value_2, currency_2, valuation_status, valuation_reason` in the default view, rounded half-even to 2 decimals per row, pre-2.6 snapshots unvalued with the reason. `GET /v2/reports/loss-value` sums stored run totals of the shown runs (reconciliation edits only in the snapshot) | engineering default under VAL03 / AN03 / API05 / WF03, not yet reviewed by the owner |

| 2026-10-10 | 3.10 Remediation runs | A remediation job or cutover step only plans; a **dry run** computes the plan in a `READ ONLY` transaction and prints it (no `migration_reports` row — so it is harmless on any database); an **apply** needs `--apply --actor "<name>" --confirm-database <connected db>` and writes, in one transaction, a `migration_reports` row, every row change guarded by its `before` values with the before / after in `migration_changes`, and its review / crosswalk / legacy-value rows tagged with the report id; a re-run skips what earlier runs recorded (idempotent); `--revert <reportId>` undoes one applied run in reverse order and is refused when a row moved since (409 `MIGRATION_ROW_CHANGED`), a review item of the run was decided (`MIGRATION_REVIEW_DECIDED`) or it was already reverted. Writable tables / columns are a fixed whitelist. Bookkeeping tables carry a nullable `tenant_id` (runs may span tenants) and no `row_version` | engineering default under D11 / MIG02, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-10 | 3.10 Remediation rules | `references`: a missing / non-tenant tenant takes the tenant owning the event's / plan's unit; no or conflicting evidence → review (`TENANT_UNRESOLVED`, `TENANT_UNIT_MISMATCH`, `TENANT_MISSING`), never a default tenant. `sentinels`: PI 9999 ends still present → open interval, raw kept; an existing raw value is never overwritten. `durations`: unknown unit or PI minutes off the timestamps by > 60 s → review. `duplicates`: same tenant / source / business id — identical (≤ 1 edited copy) → canonical (edited copy, else oldest) + crosswalk `duplicate_of` + "merge pending" review, the merge itself through the v2 merge operation after acceptance; otherwise conflict review. `legacy-values`: every non-null v1 loss / reconcile / loss-category / OEE value → `legacy_reported_values` with `method = UnknownLegacy` (OEE tenant from its plan when missing). `links`: only explicit same-tenant event ids in `reconcile_forms.productionEvent` (single, list or JSON array) → crosswalk `explains_event`; else review with the raw text. `periods`: `YYYY-MM-DD` (or `/`) = plant-local operational date → that day's generated periods; ISO instant with a zone → the containing period; anything else (month, shift name, zone-less time) → review; no period created. `reason-tree` / `rates` / `units`: report and flag only (no tree file, D08 Q3 open, no uom / currency mapping table) | engineering default under D11 / §18.2, not yet reviewed by the owner |

Valuation and exports are not compared yet (nothing legacy to compare). Delivered as CLI (`report.ts`, `cutover.ts explain`) instead of `GET /migration/compare` + admin UI: no API change | engineering default under MIG04, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-10 | 3.10 Freeze and flag switch-over | v1 write freeze per tenant (`legacy_write_freezes`, one active row): every v1 POST / PUT / PATCH / DELETE of the tenant (any `/api/*` except `/api/v2/*` and `/api/auth/*`) answers 423 `LEGACY_WRITES_FROZEN` (global interceptor, one indexed lookup per v1 write, no cache); it covers the cutover window and is released after the flags are on, because v1 admin writes (users, units, products) have no v2 screen. Cutover order `v2.codesAdmin → v2.integrationConsole → v2.plansProfilesCalendars → v2.eventHub → v2.downtimeAnalysis → v2.planDashboard → v2.plaAnalytics → v2.rca`, rollback in reverse; a flag already at the target (row or registry default) is not written. The v1 PI scheduler is paused before the freeze (it writes without HTTP). Nothing deletes or disables v1 screens / routes (C4) | engineering default under MIG05 / C4, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-10 | 3.11 JWT hardening (D15) | Access tokens carry `typ: access` and live **15 min** (`JWT_ACCESS_TTL`, default 900 s; the stale `JWT_EXPIRATION=24h` in `.env` is not read); refresh tokens carry `typ: refresh`, a `jti` and a sign-in family, are signed with `JWT_REFRESH_SECRET` (when unset or equal to `JWT_SECRET`: an HMAC of `JWT_SECRET`, warned at boot), live `JWT_REFRESH_EXPIRATION` (7 d) and are **single-use**: `auth_refresh_tokens` keeps one row per issued token (no token material). A spent token presented again within **30 s** (`JWT_REFRESH_REUSE_GRACE`, parallel tabs) gets a new pair of the same family; later it revokes the whole family (401 `REFRESH_TOKEN_REUSED`). `POST /auth/logout { refreshToken? }` revokes the family. A refresh token is never accepted as a bearer (also on sockets and `validate-token`); tokens issued before 3.11 carry no `typ` and are refused (one re-login after deploy). The legacy role and `is_active` are read from `users` on every request, so a downgrade or deactivation applies on the next request (an identity without a `users` row keeps the token's role). Expired records purged hourly by the worker. v1 frontend: a 401 is retried once after a refresh; no token (or part of one) is logged | engineering default under D15 ("just docker, jwt"); OIDC / MFA not built |
| 2026-10-10 | 3.11 `app_settings` scope | Columns `tenant_id` / `user_id` (migration `AppSettingsScopeAndRefreshTokens1793312000000`): both null = application-wide (read by every signed-in user, **written by SuperAdmin only** — branding); tenant rows read by the tenant's members, written by its TenantAdmin / manager approver acting in it (SuperAdmin / OrgAdmin included); per-user rows (`tabVisibility.<tenant>.<user>`, attributed from the key) read and written only by that user, also with a read-only role (`setValue` is `@ReadOnlyAllowed`). The retired `plantSelection` row is attributed to the tenant stored in it. An unreadable key answers as missing (empty 200, list filtered); a forbidden write is 403 `SETTING_NOT_WRITABLE`. Tenant rows are broadcast to the tenant's room only; per-user and global rows are not broadcast | engineering default (known issue 1) |
| 2026-10-10 | 3.11 Legacy sockets | `/socket.io` (v1) and `/connectivity` need `auth: { token, tenantId? }` in the handshake (same `RealtimeAuthenticator` as `/realtime`: access token only, tenant selection validated, refused with `UNAUTHENTICATED` / `FORBIDDEN` / `TENANT_REQUIRED`), and sockets are disconnected at token expiry. `/socket.io`: a socket joins its RequestContext rooms (`tenant:<id>` or `tenant:<id>:unit:<id>`); a v1 entity broadcast goes to the rooms of the entity's `tenantId` (+ `unitId`) only and is **dropped** when the payload names no tenant (no consumer exists for those); `joinEntityRoom` / `leaveEntityRoom` removed (they let any client join any room). `/connectivity`: `subscribe_tenant` only for the caller's tenants; connection / protocol rooms are not tenant-checked yet (no frontend consumer; recorded). The v1 frontend sends the token and acting tenant (`v2Session()`) and reconnects on a tenant change | engineering default (known issue 2) |
| 2026-10-10 | 3.11 Row-level security | Policy `tenant_isolation` (USING + WITH CHECK, FORCE) on every table with `tenant_id` except `organizations` (137 tables on dev): a row is visible when `app.tenant_ids` is unset / '', or its tenant is in that list, or it has no tenant (v1 global rows). The API binding (`TenantSessionBinding`, `DB_RLS_TENANT_BINDING=true`) runs on every connection checkout in the request's async context: signed-in non-SuperAdmin → `SET ROLE proact_rls_user` (NOLOGIN, NOBYPASSRLS, DML grants, created by the migration) + the acting tenant and every tenant of the caller's scopes; anything else (anonymous, SuperAdmin, worker, CLI, migrations) → `RESET ROLE` + ''. No login-credential change is needed (a non-superuser login role must be granted `proact_rls_user`). The two partition helpers are SECURITY DEFINER. Database refusals map to 403 `TENANT_NOT_ALLOWED` (42501), 400 / 422 `INVALID_REFERENCE` and 409 `RECORD_IN_USE` (23503) instead of 500. **The binding ships off**: switching it on changes what v1 users of several tenants see (member tenants only) — owner decision; the full integration suite was run with it on (evidence) | engineering default; enforcement = owner decision |
| 2026-10-10 | 3.11 v1 RCA children | Migration `RcaChildIntegrity1793398400000`: the 12 analysis tables (`*_analyses`, `*_diagrams`, v1 and `rca_*` copies) get `rca_id uuid` + FK `ON DELETE CASCADE`; `rca_knowledge_base.rca_id` becomes nullable with `ON DELETE SET NULL` (articles outlive their RCA); `logic_tree_nodes`, `rca_evidence`, `rca_recommendations` keys now cascade. Orphans (no RCA / not a uuid) are copied whole into `migration_review` (`RCA_CHILD_ORPHAN`, open for the data owner) and removed; dangling knowledge links are cleared (`RCA_LINK_CLEARED`, old value kept); a child's tenant is aligned to its RCA's (`RCA_CHILD_TENANT_ALIGNED`, before kept) and kept so by triggers (`trg_rca_child_tenant` on insert / re-link, `trg_rca_tenant_propagates` on an RCA tenant change). `down()` restores rows, tenants and links from `migration_review`. Dev: nothing orphaned or misaligned | engineering default (known issue 5) |

| 2026-10-11 | D09 MQTT integration approach | **Owner decision.** Plant broker in production, **lab broker for acceptance**; Pro-Act publishes its expectations (`docs/MQTT_OT_CONTRACT.md`) and **OT designs the topic data model** and supplies samples + schema; event identity follows standards (`event_id` + `revision`, `boot_id` + `seq`, RFC 9562 UUIDs where minted); **downtime arrives with start and end time** (interval events, PI event-frame shape; state engine 1.10 not on the MQTT critical path); messages retained (treated as re-sends, idempotent); timestamps standard (RFC 3339, NTP); mapping **AVEVA PI → MQTT → Pro-Act** to be studied field by field before build; "after insertion into the DB the message is erased" = ack-after-commit releases it from the broker, no Pro-Act spool outside the DB, raw rows kept only for the replay window. Closed 2026-10-11: D09-R = current design (ack-after-commit; the 30-day raw-row deletion was then **deferred** on 2026-10-11, "for now don't do 30 days raw retention" — raw rows kept until revisited under 3.12); D09-A = **PI → MQTT is the data engineering team's domain, Pro-Act only picks from MQTT** (no PI pull by Pro-Act for MQTT-fed units). Sequential slices of one product and unit, without concurrent streams, each starting where the previous one ends (a *product run*, e.g. month plans meeting inside a shift) are cut only by the plan: their actuals are apportioned by time, but a quality event or a micro-stop is located where it happened. The engine now records each slice's performance / quality total and claims, pools the run's claims (one per event) and allocates each to the run's slices in proportion to the slices' component totals (truncated to 8 places, remainder on the last slice; lines flagged `product_run_share`), in both ledgers. Slice totals, the capacity identity and the plan identity are unchanged; a claim beyond the run's total still fails (`IMPOSSIBLE_CLAIM`, never scaled). Pages: `/planning/plans/:planId` (version, slices, preview, generation, submit / approve / return, history), `/planning/plans/new`, `/planning/plans/:planId/versions/new`, `/planning/plans/:planId/versions/:version/edit`, `/planning/profiles/:profileId`, `/planning/profiles/new`, `/planning/profiles/:profileId/edit`, `/planning/profiles/import`, `/planning/calendars/new` (`?unitId=`, `?from=`), `/planning/calendars/:versionId/edit` (editor + preview); `/plans/:planId` is the period grid (server-paged at 20) and `/plans/:planId/periods/:periodId` the period workspace as its own page. Every list and table pages at 20. Reasons for reject / cancel / reopen / change-proposal decisions are required fields of their confirmation dialogs; retiring a profile, rate or valuation rate is confirmed. | engineering default under the owner's v2-only requirement, 2026-10-12 |
| 2026-10-12 | v1 production-plan capabilities on v2 (parity) | **Engineering default (parity).** v1 **plan templates** (unit, product, basis, weekly production-time grid) → "Start from an existing plan" on the new-plan page copies unit, basis, header target, distribution and product slices of another plan's current version; the weekly grid is superseded by the published calendar and rates by approved profiles (no template entity, no default rate). v1 **approval workflow** (submit for review, approve with comment, reject with reason) → v2 version review: new `POST /api/v2/production-plans/{id}/submit` (plan writers; draft → submitted) and `POST /api/v2/production-plans/{id}/return` (manager approvers, never the author: 403 `SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES`; reason required; submitted → draft), approve with an optional reason as before; who submitted / returned and the reason are kept on the plan header's existing `submitted_*`, `reviewed_*`, `rejection_reason` columns (no migration) and shown as `review` on the plan view (additive). v1 **comments / planner notes** → per-version change reason, approval reason and return reason (the comment thread is not rebuilt). v1 **loss reconcile / loss breakdown / unmapped events** → superseded by server accounting: loss-line candidates from events, manual lines with evidence events or "no event" + reason, the waterfall and both ledgers per period; events are attributed to periods by unit and time on the server, so there is nothing to "assign to a plan". v1 browser OEE → server KPIs (Production Loss Analysis / Reports). **Actuals adjustments** get a screen: the period workspace lists them (paged), engineers and manager approvers propose at `/plans/:planId/periods/:periodId/adjustments/new`, manager approvers who are not the author approve or reject (reason) in place. | engineering default, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-12 | Master data is v2 only; sub-pages | **Owner requirement (one version, v2) + engineering defaults.** `/master-data` is an overview (active counts from `GET /v2/master-data/summary`) with section links instead of 8 tabs: `/master-data/units`, `/units/new`, `/units/:unitId`, same for `assets` and `products`, `/master-data/hierarchy`, `/master-data/rca/templates` (+ `/new`, `/:templateId`), `/rca/causes`, `/rca/checklists` (+ `/new`, `/:checklistId`). The v1 Codes tab is a link to Codes & rules; the v1 "Users & Access" stub, "Fix DB" and "Quick Add" are gone. Lists page 20 rows (keyset, search on code and name, active / retired / all, sort by code / name / newest). New v2 routes: `GET/POST /v2/units|assets|products|rca-templates|rca-causes|verification-checklists`, `GET/PATCH /{id}` (If-Match), `POST /{id}/retire|restore` (Idempotency-Key); writers `master_data_admin`, `manager_approver` with a tenant-wide grant. Retire replaces the v1 delete everywhere (history and references stay); a unit with active assets and a cause with active sub-causes cannot be retired. Codes are unique case-insensitively per plant (units, assets), among the products a plant or its organisation sees (products), and globally (RCA causes, checklists: the v1 tables' unique keys); a duplicate is 409 `MASTER_DATA_CODE_EXISTS` on the `code` field. Migration `MasterDataV21793900500000` adds `row_version` (6 tables), `retired_at` (units, assets) and indexes. Capacity, ideal cycle time and product standard cost stay editable under "Legacy values" (not used by calculations); the standard cost is only in single product reads. | engineering default under the owner's "v2 only" requirement |
| 2026-10-12 | Products stay organisation-level | **Engineering default (kept v1 model).** A product created in Master Data belongs to the organisation of the selected plant (`tenant_id` null) unless "This plant only" is chosen. `GET /v2/products` keeps listing only the plant's own products (profiles and plans accept only those: `profile-references.ts`); `includeOrganization=true` adds the organisation's shared ones (the Master Data list) — the one v2 read that is not a plain `scopedQuery` (tenant OR (no tenant AND the plant's organisation)). The product form says so. Under row-level security a plant cannot see a sibling plant's own products, so a code clash with one is not detected for an organisation product. | engineering default, not yet reviewed by the owner |
| 2026-10-12 | RCA settings on v2 | **Engineering default (parity).** RCA templates keep their logic tree and now persist their verification checklists in `template_data` as `{ nodes, checklistIds }` (v1 read `nodes` only and lost the checklists); trees ≤ 500 nodes / 12 levels with unique node ids. The logic-tree editor uses buttons and menus (add, edit, move up / down, move under…, delete with confirmation, node forms in Radix dialogs) instead of drag-and-drop. The cause library is a lazily loaded tree (`rootsOnly`, `parentId`, `activeChildren`); moving a cause into its own subtree is 422 `CODE_CYCLE`. Export / import of templates and causes stay (JSON, client-triggered; import skips invalid or duplicate entries and reports counts). Unit / asset "PI bound" badges become a "Data source mappings" section (v2 source mappings incl. AF bindings); custom attribute values of a unit / asset are edited in a section (v1 value API until a v2 one exists; definitions are managed with the admin's attribute definitions). Bulk retire replaces bulk delete for templates and checklists. | engineering default |

| 2026-10-12 | Administration screens on v2 | **Engineering defaults (owner requirement "v2 only").** New `/api/v2` resources: `users` (members of the acting plant = home plant or a `user_scopes` grant there, plus organization-level users for OrgAdmin / SuperAdmin; `reach=everyone` SuperAdmin only; keyset by name; `manageable` flag; create / patch / delete on the v1 grant policy; a role change moves the home-plant scope row with it, so a demoted user keeps no approver scope; 409 `EMAIL_EXISTS`, 422 `CANNOT_DEACTIVATE_SELF` / `CANNOT_DELETE_SELF`), `users/directory` (any member: names for `ids` incl. inactive members and users placed in no plant, or the active members for pickers — backend item B2), `users/grantable-roles`, `user-scopes` (list / grant / revoke in the acting plant or one unit; no self grant or revoke `SELF_GRANT_NOT_ALLOWED`, a role at most the caller's rank and only for users ranked below the caller `ROLE_GRANT_NOT_ALLOWED`, the home-role mirror only via the role `HOME_SCOPE_REQUIRED`; audited by the global interceptor as `UserScope`), `tasks` (+ `summary`, `:id/complete`; overdue = due before now and neither Completed nor **Cancelled**; assignee must be an active plant member `INVALID_ASSIGNEE`), `audit-logs` (+ `summary` by action / entity / UTC day, `export` CSV ≤ 10 000 rows with formula neutralization; screen summaries drop the `via API …` tail and record ids), `custom-attribute-definitions` (plant-owned plus shared org / global rows, which only SuperAdmin changes; delete removes the values), `dashboard-configuration` (defaults served by the server without writing; reset = delete), `organizations`, `tenants` (create with IANA timezone; delete only empty plants), `branding`. Members whose home plant is another plant (cross-plant scope grants): decided 2026-10-12, row "Plant admins see users granted into their plant". UI: `/users`, `/users/new`, `/users/:userId` (details, access grants, status), `/permissions`, `/admin?tab=` (5 sections), `/admin/organizations/new`, `/admin/plants/new`, `/admin/attributes/new`, `/admin/attributes/:attributeId`, `/tasks`, `/tasks/new` (`?linkType=&linkId=` for links from other screens), `/tasks/:taskId`, `/audit`, `/audit/:entryId`; Settings reads / writes the plant through `/v2/tenants`. | engineering default, 2026-10-12 |
| 2026-10-12 | Application is v2-only | **Owner requirement (one version, v2).** Application is v2-only; v1 screens and frontend flag gates removed (`FlaggedRoute` deleted; every route serves the v2 screen); v1 backend endpoints kept until a cleanup step confirms no callers. Backend feature-flag tables and endpoints stay (`v2.rca` still selects the server trigger behaviour). Unreferenced legacy planning files (`src/pages/ProductionPlans.tsx`, `src/components/production-plans/`, `plan-dashboard-switch.tsx`, `plan-dashboard-off.tsx`, `planning-off.tsx`, `legacy/planning-link-banner.tsx`, `template/`) are left for the owner to delete. |
| 2026-10-12 | Plant admins see users granted into their plant | **Owner decision** ("Plant admin should see the users"). Plant admins see and manage access of users granted into their plant; controlled SECURITY DEFINER directory function; profile edits stay with home plant. Migration `PlantUserDirectory`: `app_plant_user_directory(p_tenant_id uuid, p_user_ids uuid[] DEFAULT NULL)` (home members of the plant or holders of a `user_scopes` row there) and `app_organization_user_by_email(p_tenant_id uuid, p_email text)` (exact, case-insensitive e-mail within the plant's organization: `users.organization_id`, else the home plant's — **assumption:** the organization of a user is that column, falling back to its home plant's organization). Both are SECURITY DEFINER with `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC and granted only to `proact_rls_user`, return only id, name, e-mail, role, `is_active`, home `tenant_id`, `organization_id`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, and raise 42501 for a tenant not in `app_rls_tenant_ids()` (the setting the RLS policies read; unrestricted sessions pass, as for the policies). The reads run in two private helpers (no EXECUTE grant) with `app.tenant_ids = ''`, so a non-superuser owner works under FORCE RLS. The API passes only the acting tenant of the request context. Used by `/v2/users`, `/v2/users/:id`, `/v2/users/directory`, task assignees, scope-grant holders and every name resolver. Profile, role, password, status and delete of a user whose home plant is another plant: 403 `HOME_PLANT_ONLY` (SuperAdmin exempt); views add `accessManageable`. First grant by e-mail: `GET /v2/users/lookup?email=` (USER_WRITERS) and `POST /v2/user-scopes { email, role, unitId? }` (`userId` or `email`, not both). UI: "From <home plant>" badge, read-only profile with a "Home plant: X" note, "Grant access by e-mail" on `/users`. | owner, 2026-10-12 |
| 2026-10-13 | Over-claims flag instead of failing the run (EXPERT_REVIEW LA-02, engine 2.5.2) | **Owner request** ("go ahead"), replaces the 2026-10-09 engineering default that a claim beyond its component fails the run. Quality events, derates or micro-stops claiming more than the computed component keep their full quantity (never scaled). The excess goes into the signed capacity residual (negative), the capacity totals are flagged `claim_exceeds_component` ("Causes claim more than measured loss"), and the run completes. The plan ledger flags the same claim `bridge_required`, so approval still waits for reconciliation. Impossible actuals (negative gross, good > gross) still fail the run. `ACCOUNTING_ENGINE_VERSION` 2.5.1 → 2.5.2, so existing runs recompute once. SME confirmation pending as EXPERT_REVIEW Q3. | owner, 2026-10-13 |
| 2026-10-13 | Baseline and calendar changes recalculate periods (LA-03) | **Owner request.** `ProfileBaselineChanged` (profile, rate, valuation rate approved / superseded / retired, rate import applied) is consumed once (`BaselineRecalcConsumer`, inbox `period-baseline`) and queues recompute jobs for the periods it covers. A profile names its unit; a rate or a profile's valuation rate is located through its profile; an impact-code valuation rate covers every unit of the tenant; an import covers its profiles' units over the window of the rates it wrote. A calendar republish with `regenerateFrom` requests recalculation of the governed units from 2 days before that date to now (margin for offset and day start). Unchanged periods keep their run (input fingerprint); in-review revisions go stale; approved ones get a change proposal. SME policy on back-dated changes pending as EXPERT_REVIEW Q7. | owner, 2026-10-13 |
| 2026-10-13 | Conservation tolerance for stored rounding (LA-12) and time-zone data check (LA-13) | **Engineering default.** The capacity identity on stored lines allows epsilon plus half a unit of the 8th place per line (`numeric(24,8)` rounding), so slices with many lines no longer fail spuriously; a real imbalance beyond that still fails. API and worker log an error at start-up when ICU tzdata still applies the abolished Asia/Tehran DST (needs tzdata ≥ 2022b; local Node 24.11 ships 2025b; images `node:24-bookworm(-slim)` and `postgres:16-alpine`). | owner, 2026-10-13 |
| 2026-10-13 | Plan targets prorated to the slice window (LA-07) | **Engineering default (no SME input needed).** A slice carries `plan_period.target × overlap(slice, plan period) / plan-period length` (`domain/accounting/slice-target.ts`); a slice equal to its plan period keeps the whole target. Before, a slice clipped by the production period (e.g. after a calendar regeneration) received the full target, so a target could count twice. Ships in engine 2.5.2. | owner, 2026-10-13 |
| 2026-10-13 | Settled periods with a stop measured open are recomputed once (DT-08) | **Engineering default.** The period sweep also queues settled, not-reconciled periods whose current draft or in-review run measured an open event to the run instant (`input_fingerprint.asOf` set), so the stop is measured to the period end. The next run has no `asOf`, so the period is queued once. | owner, 2026-10-13 |
| 2026-10-13 | Loss-value report shows the approved, reconciled value (VA-01) | **Engineering default (correctness).** Approval writes the reconciled plan-ledger valuation per slice, kind, view and currency into the new immutable table `approved_valuation_totals` (migration `ApprovedValuationTotals1793906000000`, RLS `tenant_isolation`), computed with the same aggregator as the run (not part of the reviewed content, so submitted hashes are unchanged). `GET /v2/reports/loss-value` reads these for approved revisions on the plan ledger, else the run totals (draft, capacity ledger, revisions approved before the migration); `basis` text updated. Verified: report now IRR 150 000 000 / USD 300 = approval snapshot (was 90 000 000 / 180). RCA approved impact uses the same loss kinds (no `schedule_exclusion`). **Open (VA-01b):** the loss tree and RCA approved impact still read run candidates, not reconciled decisions. | owner, 2026-10-13 |
| 2026-10-13 | Micro-stop test uses the whole occurrence (DT-09, engine part) | **Engineering default.** The engine receives the full extent of each stop occurrence (already loaded for the KPIs); a split child inside the window is a micro-stop only when the whole occurrence is shorter than `microStopSeconds`. **Deferred:** rejecting future start / end on manual entry needs an injectable clock first (the integration world is dated March 2027). | owner, 2026-10-13 |
| 2026-10-13 | v1 RCA write routes need the v2 RCA roles (RCA-02) | **Engineering default (security; the v1 routes stay while callers remain, per 2026-10-12).** Every POST / PUT / PATCH / DELETE of the v1 RCA controllers now carries `@Roles`: case work (`/rcas`, the six analysis tools and `/rca-analysis/*`, logic-tree nodes, evidence and evidence uploads, recommendations, knowledge-base writes) needs `RCA_WRITERS` (reliability engineer, manager approver); RCA libraries (cause codes, templates, evidence categories / locations, analysis configs) need `MASTER_DATA_WRITERS`; `/rca-trigger-configs` needs `manager_approver` like the v2 RCA policy. Default and switching stay with the owner. | owner, 2026-10-13 |

Reject stays refused only for the submitter. The Plan Dashboard disables Approve for line authors with the reason. | owner, 2026-10-13 |

| 2026-10-13 | Fault-tree top-event probability (RCA-06) | **Engineering default.** The saved fault tree carries `topEventProbability` (independent basic events: AND = Π p, OR = 1 − Π(1 − p), an intermediate event takes its single input) or null with `topEventProbabilityReason` (`probability_missing`, `no_single_top_event`, `not_a_tree`, `gate_without_inputs`, `ambiguous_event`); shown under the tool. | 2026-10-13 | Database performance batch B4 (DB-01, 02, 04, 06, 07, 10) | **Engineering defaults.** (1) GiST `IDX_production_events_unit_span` on `(unit_id, tstzrange(start_time, end_time, '[)'))` for live events; the accounting, KPI and actuals loaders use `tstzrange(...) && tstzrange($from, $to)` (same rows: `CHK_production_events_interval` end > start). | 2026-10-13 | SME answers to EXPERT_REVIEW Area 1 (Q1–Q18) | **SME decision** (recorded verbatim in summary in `plans/EXPERT_REVIEW.md` §1.2, impact in §1.4). | 2026-10-13 | Manual event times may not be in the future (DT-09) | **Engineering default.** v2 create, and a v2 PATCH that changes the interval, refuse a start or end later than now + 120 s (422 `IN_FUTURE` with the field; the allowance covers browser/server clock difference). Nothing is merged or excluded automatically: KPIs are unchanged until the SME sets the merge rule (D5). |

Migration `KpiScheduleDowntime` adds both `kpi_metric` values. `downtime_total` and the Pareto stay on the calendar window, now labelled so; whether the Pareto moves to scheduled time is SME D14. |
| 2026-10-13 | Lost production by stop reason from the ledger (FE-08) | **Engineering default.** No new server metric: `GET /v2/reports/loss-tree` gains an optional repeated `kinds` filter (loss kinds of the tree; additive contract change, OpenAPI snapshot and frontend types updated). Micro-stop and derate (performance) losses are not in it; the downtime-duration Pareto is unchanged. |
| 2026-10-13 | Faster stop coding (FE-06, FE-07) | **Engineering default (no API change).** Inline "Code" (uncoded rows of the Event Hub list, current stops on Event Hub and Downtime Analysis) saves only `reasonCodeId` with PATCH + `If-Match`, so the server applies the code's classification defaults. The close dialog has an optional reason: a new code is saved first (PATCH), then the stop is closed with the returned row version; if the close is refused the code stays. "To code" sets the list to uncoded downtime of the same units and window; the select column header ticks every listed row. **Open for the owner (with SME D13):** whether bulk coding may go without a written "Why" (today the server requires it on every operation), and whether a bulk-by-filter API (code every match, not only the loaded rows) is wanted. |
