| 2026-10-15 | Economic view breakdown by component (VA-04) | **Engineering default.** The economic parts of every valued line are stored with its normalized valuation row per reporting currency (`loss_line_valuations.components`, `approved_loss_line_valuations.components`; converted exactly like the line, so a line's parts add up to its amount). `GET /reports/loss-value?groupBy=component` (economic view only) sums them in SQL per component and currency over the shown periods — approved revision, else the open draft, like the other management reports — with status valued / mixed (some line without an FX rate: no amount, never a partial sum) / unvalued, provisional-FX flag and tonnes per unit. Immutable rows written before valuation 2.8.0 are not back-filled: they appear as `not_broken_down` (recalculating an open period, or restating an approved one, breaks them down); lines without a value appear as `unvalued` with their reasons. The browser shows the server's figures only (no sums). |
| 2026-10-15 | Rework yield loss and displaced bottleneck contribution (SME V2 remainder) | **Engineering default per SME V2, to confirm (FIN / MET).** Valuation 2.8.0, economic view of a rework line = three non-overlapping parts: (1) `rework_cost` × reworked tonnes; (2) **yield loss** = tonnes not recovered, valued like scrap (lost contribution only when demand is constrained, + variable cost − salvage + disposal); recorded E3 recovery movements of the line's rework lots decide (reworked − recovered, time-share aligned, scaled to the line), the approved **`rework_yield`** of the profile only for the unrecorded rest (new valuation rate basis: ratio 0 < y ≤ 1, no currency, finance maintainer / approver); no yield and no recovery → no yield-loss part, note `no_rework_yield_rate` (not guessed); (3) **displaced contribution** = rework time × the profile's approved planning rate × contribution, only where the product's demand status is constrained (or the policy charges all lost output) at the rework time: recorded reprocessing on the lot's own unit uses its interval; reprocessing **remeasured** at the boundary is already lost output of its own period's ledger → 0 (`displaced_output_in_ledger`); reprocessing on another unit → 0 (`displaced_rework_other_unit`); unrecorded rework is assumed to run at the planned rate, displacing its own tonnes (`rework_time_estimated`, replaced when the recovery is approved — approval now also recalculates the original lot's periods); not constrained → 0 (`displaced_not_constrained`). Worked example (fixtures): 10 t, $300 contribution, $700 variable, $50 salvage, $20 disposal, $60 rework, yield 0.9, 5 t/h, sold out → $600 + $970 + $3,000 = $4,570; recorded 8.5 t back in 1.5 h → $600 + $1,455 + $2,250 = $4,305; spare capacity → $1,270. All parts priced at the line's valuation date. Under the demand-constrained rule a rework line now needs an approved demand status of its product (as scrap does), else it stays unvalued `demand_status_missing`. Older runs keep their stored 2.7.0 values. |
| 2026-10-15 | Product dispositions (E3), Quality approver, provisional FX, bad actors | **Owner: standard and common practice.** E3: first-pass good, wide-spec, downgrade, transition off-grade, scrap, rework kept apart (lot dispositions, aligned to the production interval); **accepted rework is not good output** (OEE / ISO 22400 first-pass quality; replaces the D05 add-back — periods with rework adjustments show lower good on recalculation); **recovered output is reported, not credited against the plan** (net fresh production counted once; recovered value stays in valuation); measured rejects beat lot classes (slice flagged); actuals 2.4.2, accounting 2.5.7. **Disposition decisions belong to Quality / Lab (ISO 9001):** new scope role `quality_approver` approves / rejects; production, accounting, managers and quality record; author never decides (demo persona Parisa Rahimi). Provisional FX (V7): a finance-authorised provisional rate fills dates without a final rate (end date required), values flagged everywhere incl. CSV `fx_provisional`; the final rate wins and revalues open periods / proposes changes for approved ones; a provisional value never fires the D17 money limit; valuation 2.7.0. Loss tree, RCA impact and the D17 trigger use the policy default view. Bad-actor report: monthly, RCA policy window, plant-calendar month (Jalali on Persian plants). Packages dropped: node-opcua, modbus-serial, ethernet-ip, serialport, profinet, scada, sqlite3. |
| 2026-10-15 | Metrology approver, source authority maker-checker, standby exposure in RCA effectiveness | **Owner: standard and common practice.** (1) New scope role `metrology_approver` (Metrology / Instrumentation approver, rank 60): uncertainty budgets are approved or rejected only by the metrology / instrumentation owner (ISO/IEC 17025 / GUM practice), never the author; authors stay production engineering, integration, production accounting and managers, plus the metrology approver; adopting a budget as the unit's tolerance stays production accounting / manager approver, never the budget author. Demo persona Kaveh Ahmadi (Metrology) in both demo plants. (2) Source authority versions are maker-checker: `POST /v2/source-authority/versions` stores a draft (integration admin or production accounting) that resolves nowhere; production accounting approves (never the author) and the version is in force from the approval instant, ending the scope's previous version; a draft prepared from a version no longer in force is refused (409 `STALE_DRAFT`); a rejection needs a reason; calculated periods keep their version until recalculated. Migration `1794180030000-SourceAuthorityMakerChecker`; screen `/connectivity/source-authority`: a hierarchy editor drafts a unit or plant-wide version from the one in force (ranked sources at a named boundary, fallback conditions, cross-boundary declaration with stock change, lag and evidence; the server validates and its refusal is shown), and the version list shows the unit's and the plant-wide versions with their scope so either can be decided there (`includeTenantWide` list filter). Fixture RCA04. |

| 2026-10-14 | SME answers R7–R11 (manual loss lines) built — phase R-A | **SME decision** (SME_ANSWERS Part B; numbers are pilot settings). | 2026-10-13 | Reporting week of Iranian plants starts on Saturday (DT-10, SME D16) | **Owner decision** ("in Iran the weekend is Thursday and Friday, so the first day should be Saturday"). New plant setting `tenants.week_start` (`monday` ISO 8601 \| `saturday`); migration `TenantWeekStart` sets Saturday where the plant or a site is in Asia/Tehran or the plant uses the Persian calendar; a new plant without a value gets the same default (`defaultWeekStart`). Week buckets (downtime / OEE SQL reports, reconciliation trend) use it: Monday weeks keep the ISO key `YYYY-"W"WW`, Saturday weeks are keyed by their Saturday `YYYY-MM-DD` and shown as "Week from <date>" in the plant calendar (Solar Hijri weeks have no ISO number). Editable in Plant settings ("Reporting week") and `PATCH /v2/tenants/{id}/display-settings` (`weekStart`, additive). Which days are worked (the Thursday–Friday weekend) stays the shift calendar's job. |
| 2026-10-13 | A, P and Q over the periods their OEE counts (DT-10) | **Engineering default (ISO 22400 consistency).** In grouped OEE reports a period's availability / performance / quality count only where its OEE (reconciled family: `reconciled_oee`) is complete, so the summed terms chain (ΣR/ΣS · ΣI/ΣR · ΣG/ΣI = ΣG/ΣS) and A × P × Q = OEE over any set of periods; periods left out (e.g. no actuals yet) make the figure provisional (`incomplete_periods`). Per-period values are unchanged. |
| 2026-10-15 | Month close (SME R1 / R4) built — phase R-B | **SME decision, built; engineering defaults to confirm (PA / FIN).** One close per plant (tenant) and financial month (`month_closes`), states open → quantities closed → finance signed off → locked; policy `month_close` (effective-dated, seeded per plant on first use): quantities by **working day 3**, finance by **working day 5** after month end, open change proposals block the close. **Engineering defaults:** (1) Iranian plants (site calendar Asia/Tehran or Persian display calendar) close **Solar Hijri** months with a **Thursday–Friday** weekend, others Gregorian with Saturday–Sunday; (2) a working day is any non-weekend date that is not a holiday of the default site's published calendar (both holiday treatments count as non-working); a deadline ends at local midnight after the due day; (3) roles: `manager_approver` closes quantities, `finance_approver` signs off and locks, never the same person (403 SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES, also for one user holding both roles); either signer may reopen a closing month (all sign-offs cleared); a locked month is never reopened; (4) prerequisites: every period of the month (by operational date, all units) has an approved latest revision — an excluded period never reconciled is skipped; (5) lock: a direct revise of a period in a locked month is 409 `MONTH_LOCKED`; accepting a change proposal opens the correction, marked `afterClose` on the revision; a closing (not yet locked) month refuses both (409 `MONTH_CLOSING`); (6) materiality routing is a policy option, **off**: thresholds per unit of measure on Σ\|bridge + classified Unaccounted + tolerance adjustment\| of the month's approved ledger; when reached, a reviewer of the policy's `reviewerRoles` (default `production_accountant`, independent of both signers) confirms before finance. Open: a correction of a locked month without a system change proposal (manual proposal) is not built; the month close needs a tenant-wide grant. |
| 2026-10-15 | Reconciliation SLA, waiting for data, rejection (SME R3 / R6) built — phase R-C | **SME decision, built; engineering defaults to confirm (OPS / PA).** Policy `reconciliation_sla` (tenant-wide, effective-dated, seeded on first use): submit ≤ 12 h and approve ≤ 24 h after the **production day** ends, escalate at 48 h to `production_accountant` and at 72 h to `manager_approver`, the 3rd rejection of the same revision to `production_accountant`. **Engineering defaults:** (1) the production day ends at the latest period end of the unit and operational date; (2) after a rejection the corrected submission is due 12 h after it (never before the first due time) and approval 12 h after that; (3) "waiting for data" is a hold per period (kind lab / source / other, reason required, one open at a time, not on an approved period) that adds its time to both due times and leaves it out of the age, so a hold never makes a period overdue or escalates it further; holds are opened and released by period writers; (4) escalation is shown (grid, period, report, "My queue"), no e-mail or push notification yet; (5) rejections count per revision ("version lineage" = the revision; a revise starts a new count); failed lines are line ids or candidate auto keys of the reviewed revision; (6) the status report's `overdueAfterHours` parameter is removed (a request that still sends it is refused by validation) — overdue = past the current stage's due time; (7) "My queue" lists ended, not approved periods of the last 62 operational days the caller can prepare (period writers), review (revision approvers, not their own submission) or that escalated to one of their roles, earliest due first; (8) the frontend's period approval now follows `REVISION_APPROVERS` (production accounting approves too; adjustments stay with manager approvers) — the split announced in the R-A row. |
| 2026-10-15 | Restatement delta, posting date, source status mapping (SME R5 / R12 / R13) built — phase R-D | **SME decision, built; engineering defaults to confirm (PA / FIN / MET).** (1) R5: a restatement is any revision that supersedes an approved one (opened by revise or an accepted change proposal); its delta compares the reviewed content of both revisions — plan-ledger totals and actuals per unit of measure, loss lines grouped by kind and reason code (unchanged groups hidden); the operational effective date is the period's operational date; `finance_approver` sets a posting date and a treatment (`correction`, `estimate_change`, `other_adjustment`, `no_posting`), a later decision replaces the earlier one (audited); no general-ledger export. (2) R12 verified: Reports default to approved (built 14 Oct); the API keeps the shown revision as default for dashboards. (3) R13: `source_status_mappings` per tenant and source system (`import` = API / file imports; `sourceSystem` is a new optional field / CSV column of external reports); a version applies once approved by `production_accountant` (the source owner for production records, engineering default) who is not its author; drafts by `integration_admin` or `production_accountant`; eligibility is decided at import with the version in force then and kept on the record (`status_mapping_id`) — a later mapping version does not re-evaluate stored records; existing plants got `import` v1 from `EXTERNAL_REPORT_CERTIFIED_STATUSES` (default COMPLETED, RELEASED) and new plants get it on first use — **the source owner should replace it** (the SME notes COMPLETED may not mean released); any other source releases nothing until its mapping is approved. Open: no UI for mappings yet (API only). |
| 2026-10-15 | Day certification (SME R2, owner choice) built — phase R-E | **Owner decision (14 Oct), built; engineering defaults to confirm (OPS / PA).** Shifts are prepared and approved per period as before; one approval per production day of a unit certifies the roll-up (`production_day_certifications`, the approved revisions it covers and a hash of the roll-up; quantities are never stored a second time). **Engineering defaults:** (1) the certifier holds `manager_approver` and submitted none of the day's shifts (403 SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES); (2) a day is ready when every period that needs approval (all but excluded, never reconciled ones) has an approved latest revision; (3) un-certify only through the correction path: any revise of a shift (direct or by an accepted change proposal, also after month close) reopens the day with the revise reason; a new certification is a new row; (4) the month close requires every unit-day of the month with periods to be certified (`month_close.requireCertifiedDays`, default true for new versions, false for a version written without the field); (5) the day is per unit — no plant-wide day roll-up across units (different products and units of measure). Demo seeder: previous month's ready days certified, the previous month closed and locked when its prerequisites hold, one ready day of the current month certified, one draft shift waiting for the lab. |
| 2026-10-15 | Area 3 leftovers: manual change proposal, source status rules screen, escalation notifications | **Built; engineering defaults to confirm (PA / OPS / FIN).** (1) Manual change proposal (SME R4): `production_accountant` or `manager_approver` propose a correction of the published approved revision of a period in a **closing or locked** month (`POST /v2/period-change-proposals`, trigger `manual_correction`, reason + evidence type and reference required); an open month refuses it (409 `MONTH_OPEN`: revise directly); one open proposal per approved revision (409 `PROPOSAL_OPEN`); the proposer never accepts **or dismisses** it (403 `SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES`, every role); acceptance follows the system path (closing month 409 `MONTH_CLOSING`, locked month → revise draft `afterClose`, restatement delta and posting decision as R5); a later system change refreshes the preview of an open manual proposal but keeps its trigger, reason and evidence; there is no withdraw — another approver dismisses it. (2) Source status rules screen (R13) at `/connectivity/source-status`, listed for every analysis role; the list API names the version **in force now** (approved, interval contains now, highest version) and author / approver names. (3) Escalation notifications (R3 / R6): in-app only — table `user_notifications` (per user and plant, read / unread), worker job every **10 minutes** evaluates the queue window (62 days) with the `reconciliation_sla` policy in force; one notification per user, period and age level (`sla:<period>:age:<n>`, a period first seen at level 2 also notifies level 1's role) and per user and revision for the 3rd rejection; recipients = active users holding the escalation role on the plant, the unit's site or the unit (scope grants) or by a legacy home-plant role that maps to it (TenantAdmin / Manager → `manager_approver`); SuperAdmin / OrgAdmin are not addressees; a realtime hint (outbox `UserNotificationsCreated`, tenant room, no content) refreshes the header bell. **No e-mail or SMS** (no delivery infrastructure). |
| 2026-10-15 | DB-05 finished: core v1 `tenant_id` to uuid, orphan rows | **Engineering default (standard practice).** Migration `1794070000000-CoreTenantUuid` converts production_events, audit_logs, production_plans, assets, products, pi_connections, rcas with every RCA analysis table, rca_knowledge_base, rca_analysis_configs and rca_trigger_configs to `uuid` with a FK to `tenants`; the production_events `tenant_uuid` shadow column and its trigger are dropped (readers use `tenant_id`). **Orphans are never deleted to satisfy a FK:** a table whose rows name an unknown tenant gets the FK `NOT VALID` (new rows are still checked) and the migration lists the orphan ids in a NOTICE. audit_logs (314 rows on dev, 96 deleted test tenants): kept; the 30-day audit retention removes them, then `ALTER TABLE audit_logs VALIDATE CONSTRAINT "FK_6f18d459490bb48923b1f40bdb7"`. production_plans (7 rows on dev): business data, kept for the data owner to re-assign or delete; validate `FK_0a60dfceafde9f802bdf5443304` once clean. A value that is not a UUID keeps its table (the RCA family as one group) varchar with a WARNING. Consequence: a tenant with audit rows or plans can no longer be hard-deleted (409 RECORD_IN_USE); the audit interceptor writes a missing tenant as NULL instead of ''. |
| 2026-10-15 | Area 2 leftovers: standby MTBF exposure, reliability inputs recalculate, master data in the run hash | **Engineering default (ISO 14224 / SME D9 consistency), to confirm (MNT).** (1) KPI engine 2.7.7: the asset MTBF of an asset registered as standby equipment at any instant of the period uses its own running time (union of its `standby_records` runs clipped to the window, open runs to the run instant) over its D13 failures that **started while it was running**; failures outside its runs (failed start, dormant failure found on demand) stay failure on demand in the standby measures and are only counted in the cohort (`failuresOutsideRunning`); zero failures → null with the exposure, no runs → null `no_running_exposure`; standby assets get asset KPIs even without failures. The unit MTBF and other assets keep the unit run time. (2) CMMS operation imports (periods of the linked stops' occurrences, old and new links), standby run imports (old and new run spans), standby duty register / retire and state-mapping versions (from their start, future periods included) write `PeriodRecalculateRequested` with the change; late samples of a unit's mapped state signal are picked up by the 5-min period sweep (stored after the period's last completed recompute) — one outbox message per streamed sample would flood the outbox. Idempotent through the inbox, collapsed queued jobs and the input hash. (3) The run input hash includes each event's asset type and reason code (the values the micro-stop threshold and trip rule read through ids); renaming / re-typing does **not** queue recalculations by itself (like `oee_policy` versions: no mass restatement of approved months from a master-data edit) but the next recalculation of a period writes a new run instead of keeping the old one. The failure dictionary does not affect classification (checked on user writes only), so it is not hashed. Old runs keep their stored hash and engine version. No migration, no API change. |

Stored with calibration evidence and next review date, effective-dated (approving a later budget ends the one in force), maker-checker. The zero default stays; residuals are always published. **Engineering choices for the owner:** (1) no `metrology` role exists — production / process engineering, integration admins, production accounting and managers record, **production accounting approves** (add a metrology role later if wanted); (2) adoption by production accounting or managers; (3) the budget's unit is not converted or checked against the slice unit — the justification shows the budget's unit; (4) a percent tolerance is not derived from a budget (it needs a denominator choice). | 2026-10-15 | State engine and stop reconciliation: derived stops, duplicate merge, repeat-stop incidents (plan 1.10, DT-04, SME D1 / D2 / D5) | **Engineering default (standard practice, owner 15 Oct: "standard and common practice"), to confirm (OPS / MNT / automation).** (1) **Derived stops**: for every unit with a state mapping in force the worker (every minute, per-unit advisory lock) derives downtime events from the SME D1 / D2 state timeline: internal stop → `unplanned`, planned stop / changeover → `planned`, starved / blocked → `external` (SME D3: cause outside the unit), standby → `planned` (inside scheduled time it still lowers availability, row "Reliability plant-data items"); running and not scheduled produce nothing; **unknown time is never a stop** — it ends the known part of a stop (description "end at the last known sample") and a stop resuming after it is a second event of the same occurrence (the gap is not filled). DT02 pilot values: a stop occurrence shorter than 5 s is chatter, running shorter than 10 s between stop samples is not a restart (the confirmation delay is never added). Events carry `source_kind = derived`, `source = State engine`, business id `state:<unit>:<state>@<start ms>`; reprocessing is idempotent (per-unit cursor; a late or corrected sample reopens the window from its observation time; a changed end is a source revision, a gone key is retracted, a key a person split / merged / retracted is never written again). Lookback 24 h (`STATE_ENGINE_LOOKBACK_HOURS`). No `equipment_state_segments` table: the timeline stays computed from samples (one source of truth with the coverage KPIs). (2) **Source authority / duplicate merge (DT-04, SRC02)**: two live stopping events (downtime, impact not `none` / `fractional`) of one unit, the same asset (or one unit-level), overlapping, start within the tolerance and — both closed — end within it are one stop recorded twice. Timing authority state engine → source frame (PI / MQTT) → import → manual; cause authority operator → source → import → engine. A unique cross-source pair without a cause conflict merges automatically: the kept stop keeps its own timing and fills only its empty cause fields (reason, asset, codes, origin, failure detail; operator first), the other is superseded with a `correlated_with` relation and joins its occurrence — so nothing is counted twice and the duplicate stays as evidence. Same-source pairs, different non-empty causes or several equally ranked candidates are suggestions; accept / reject / undo by any event writer with a reason (revisions with the user as actor). Undo restores the evidence in its own occurrence and clears copied values nobody changed since; rejected / undone pairs are never proposed again. Pilot tolerance 120 s (no SME number; D5 asks to deduplicate independently of repeat grouping), automatic merge on. (3) **Incidents (SME D5, S3)**: stops of one unit, asset (or unit-level) and reason starting ≤ 120 s (pilot) after the previous stop of the chain ended form one incident; stops without a reason are never grouped, an open stop ends a chain, a person's "new failure" mark (verified repair before the stop) breaks it. Stops stay separate events with their own durations (stopped time = the stops, not the envelope). KPI engine **2.7.8**: MTBF counts an incident once at its start (an incident begun in an earlier window is not counted again; cohort `repeatStopsInIncidents`); mean time to restore and MTTR stay per stop; the incident id is in the run input hash and a changed grouping requests recalculation of its span. Old runs keep their engine version and hash. (4) Settings are the `stopEvents` option of `oee_policy` (effective-dated versions, unit override; absent = the pilot defaults above): `deriveFromState`, `debounceSeconds`, `restartConfirmSeconds`, `duplicateToleranceSeconds`, `autoMergeDuplicates`, `incidentWindowSeconds`. Not built (1.10 remainder): unit impact rules / `event_impacts`, the overlap `CauseAllocator` (2.5 first-out stays), analog thresholds with hysteresis, reduced-rate segments, source authority fallback banners for actuals. Migration `1794170000000-StopEventsStateEngine`; new routes `/v2/stop-reconciliations`, `/v2/stop-incidents`, `/v2/stop-event-policies`, `/v2/derived-stops/preview`; `incidentId` added to the v2 event read model. |
| 2026-10-15 | Transition-standard key with catalyst family and operating mode (SME E1) built — accounting 2.5.8 | **SME decision, built; engineering defaults to confirm (OPS / process engineering).** The matrix key is unit × from × to × catalyst family × operating mode (`transition_standards.catalyst_family` / `operating_mode`, migration `1794160000000-TransitionStandardContext`); each key is its own version family (maker-checker, no overlapping approved versions per full key). Defaults: (1) a blank catalyst family / operating mode on a standard means **any**; (2) the **most specific** approved entry in force at the transition start wins, and on a tie **catalyst family outranks operating mode** (the catalyst system sets the transition chemistry); (3) the grade-transition event supplies the context (`production_events.catalyst_family` / `operating_mode`, grade_transition only, else 422 `INVALID_GRADE_CHANGE`); without an operating mode on the event, the production context applies: the operating mode of the profile the to-product's slice runs on (`normal` today); a missing catalyst family stays unknown and only matches "any" entries; (4) codes are free text of the profile operating-mode shape (1–64 letters, digits, `_ . -`) — a catalyst-family code list can follow when the plant supplies one. Existing standards and events have no context and match exactly as in 2.5.7. |
| 2026-10-15 | Concurrent products by stream, run and lot (SME E6 / Q17, LA-10) built — accounting 2.6.0, actuals 2.4.4 | **SME decision, built; engineering defaults to confirm (OPS / PA / process engineering).** D14's "sequential only" no longer limits R1: a unit may have **streams** (`unit_streams`, maker-checker master data per unit and code: name and share of the unit's rate, effective-dated; the approved streams of a unit never provide more than its whole rate, 409 `STREAM_SHARE_EXCEEDED`) and **stream runs** (`stream_runs`: which product a stream ran over `[start, end)` and the lot it made; operational record by period writers, no overlap per stream, a lot code made once per tenant). Migration `1794160100000-UnitStreams`. Defaults: (1) with runs recorded in a period, a product slice's capacity is Σ stream share × rate × the time that stream ran the product (two trains 60 / 40 % at 50 t/h for 8 h → 240 t + 160 t = 400 t, never 800 t); a product no stream ran gets 0, flagged `no_stream_run`; stream time with no run carries no capacity; (2) **no runs in the period = the 2.5.8 single-stream model** (concurrent slices still flagged `concurrent_products`), so existing units and old runs reproduce exactly; one stream at share 1 gives the same lines as no streams; (3) an event may name a stream (`streamCode`): a stop of one train is charged only to the product that train ran; an event without a stream acts on every stream; (4) declared quantities (quality loss, measured slowdowns, transition off-grade) are assigned once: a stream's to the product it ran, a unit-wide one split by each product's stream capacity while it lasted (10 t over 60 / 40 % trains → 6 t + 4 t); (5) outputs: a lot-coded output (`lotCode`) belongs to the run that made the lot, over that run's interval (E6 example: lot A bagged at 14:00 after the reactor switched to B counts for A, not B; E3 production-interval alignment); a stream output goes to the product that stream ran, split by time across a product change (marked estimated, mass conserved to 8 decimals); unit-level output stays unattributable while products share a window (as before); (6) the KPI engine still combines concurrent slices as "not combined" (`concurrent_streams`), unchanged. Not built: mixture composition of transition material, stream-level KPIs, recompute of a lot's earlier periods when its output is measured later (picked up when those periods recompute). |
| 2026-10-15 | Plant flow and buffers (SME E2, with Q13) built — accounting 2.7.0 | **SME decision, built; engineering defaults to confirm (OPS / process engineering / PA).** A directed material-flow map (`plant_flow_routes`, maker-checker master data per from → to unit, effective-dated, no overlapping approved version, never a cycle: 422 `FLOW_CYCLE`): route role (feed / alternate), conversion yield (recorded on the map), the buffer between the units (name, usable inventory, lowest / highest operating level, inventory unit) and the **downstream draw per hour** that turns inventory into cover; recorded buffer levels (`buffer_levels`). Migration `1794160200000-PlantFlow`; screen *Master data → Plant flow*; `GET /v2/reports/site-loss`. Defaults: (1) an upstream stop (recorded stopping downtime, SME Q5) first consumes the buffer's cover = usable ÷ draw; only the rest starves the downstream unit (SME example: 100 t at 20 t/h = 5 h; a 2 h stop costs nothing, a 7 h stop 2 h = 40 t), and starvation ends when the upstream stop ends (no refill or ramp-up model yet); (2) usable = the latest recorded level not older than **24 h** at the stop's start, minus the lowest operating level; else the configured usable inventory (else highest − lowest level; no buffer = immediate starvation); each upstream stop uses the level at its own start (no partial-refill tracking between close stops); (3) starvation propagates through chains (A → B → C, up to 8 levels) and keeps the originating stop as cause; with **alternate** routes the unit starves only while every alternate's upstream is out too; (4) the derived starvation is an **availability loss of the downstream unit, schedule class external, cause = the upstream stop** (reason code and evidence of that stop), flagged `upstream_starvation` and `origin_unit:<id>`; the downstream unit's own recorded stops are facts and take precedence (derived time only outside them); the KPI engine keeps recorded stops only; (5) upstream stops are read 7 days back; a stop's change recalculates the downstream units over its window; a route or level change recalculates the downstream unit; (6) site consolidation (Q13: origin, impact, consequence): the site loss is the sum of the units that feed no other unit at the period start (the boundary); upstream units keep their own loss as impact but are not added again, so a stop counts once — where it reaches the boundary — by origin. Not modelled yet: refill / partial buffers between close stops, shared utilities, capacity of alternate routes (an alternate is assumed to cover fully), blocking (downstream stop filling an upstream buffer). |
| 2026-10-15 | Plan 1.10 remainder: unit impact rules, one cause allocator, analog thresholds, actuals fallback banner (SME Q5 / D7 / D9, DT01, SRC01) — KPI 2.7.10 | **Engineering default (owner 15 Oct: "standard and common practice"), to confirm (OPS / MNT / PE / automation / PA).** (1) **Unit impact rules** (`unit_impact_rules`, maker-checker per unit and asset, effective-dated, no overlapping approved version): `full_stop` / `fractional` (share of the unit rate lost, 0 < f ≤ 1) / `none`, optional installed standby. No rule = the stop stops the unit (unchanged). **Standby takeover** = a logged successful standby run (SME D9 log) starting within the **takeover window (pilot 60 s)** after the stop's start and lasting at least to its end → impact `none` (failure without production loss, SME Q5); a late, failed or shorter run keeps the rule's own impact for the whole stop (the switchover gap is not charged separately). The derived impact is written onto the event (system coding revision, `impact_basis` `unit_rule` / `standby_takeover`; `event_impacts` keeps rule version and standby run), so every reader uses one impact. **Manual override:** an impact a person states (v2 create / patch with `impactMode`) is `stated` and never replaced; events written before the basis existed count as stated when their impact is anything but `full_stop`. Writers: master data, reliability engineers, manager approvers; approver: a manager approver other than the author. An approved or retired rule re-resolves the asset's stops **starting** in its validity; standby run imports re-resolve the stops around the runs. (2) **CauseAllocator:** the ledgers and the KPI breakdowns share one allocation (one cause per occurrence; first-out default since S1, equal share for older `oee_policy` versions); no result changed. Upstream starvation keeps its own chain rule (first upstream stop keeps the merged outage); manual weights are not modelled — corrections go through reconciliation lines. (3) **Analog thresholds:** stopped below `stopBelow`, reduced rate below `reducedBelow`, running above; hysteresis default 0; an analog stop maps to `internal_stop` unless the mapping says starved / blocked; the first sample (and the one before a window) uses plain thresholds; text rules win over thresholds. `reduced_rate` is running time (coverage and availability bounds count it as running, KPI 2.7.10) and never a stop; reduced-rate spans ≥ the debounce (5 s pilot), with restarts shorter than the confirmation (10 s) inside them, become slowdown events (performance, unplanned): with the signal's rated value a derate of 1 − time-weighted mean / rated (8 places), without one an unquantified `measured` slowdown (no claim until quantified). A person's stated impact on a slowdown is never overwritten. (4) **Fallback banner (step 5):** `SourceFallbackActivated` / `SourceFallbackEnded` per product when the E7 selection moves to a substitute or back (from the unit's preceding period, once per change, a withdrawn fallback ended by the recalculation), the return carrying the run's `source_conflict` (primary kept); in-app notification `source_fallback` to production accounting and integration of the unit. No sub-slice source switching (one source per slice, E7). Migrations `1794190100000-UnitImpactRules`, `1794190200000-AnalogStateThresholds`, `1794190300000-SourceFallbackNotifications`; routes `/v2/unit-impact-rules` (+ approve / retire); `thresholds` on unit state mappings; `slowdowns` in `GET /v2/derived-stops/preview`; `classification.impactBasis` on the v2 event. Old runs keep their engine version. |

For 1.7: which tags hold production actuals for unit100–unit500 (output frames, totalisers or rates; units and timebase) — per tag: WebId or path, semantic type, engineering unit, rate timebase (t/h vs t/d), totaliser rollover value (modulus) and whether/when it resets (shift, day, campaign, meter change), the maximum credible rate, the archive/scan cadence and how long a gap may be before data counts as missing.

For 1.7: the timebase PI assumes for `Total` summaries of each rate tag (AF UOM / point setting; PI's default is per day), whether stream updates (`streams/{id}/updates`) are enabled and how long markers stay valid, and how system digital states (`No Data`, `Shutdown`, `I/O Timeout`, …) and questionable/substituted values appear on these tags.
  12. For 1.7: whether archive values are edited or removed after the fact (corrections, `Remove` update events) and how far back.

## 2026-10-18 — UX audit remediation decisions (`docs/UX_AUDIT.md`, plan `plans/UX_REMEDIATION.md`)

Owner answers:
- **Open stop label:** an event whose state is `open` displays as **"Ongoing"** (e.g. "Ongoing 59 h 04 min"), never "Running". State values unchanged.
- **Sidebar:** keep the 2026-10-17 expandable Downtime/PLA sections. Fix only truncated labels, duplicate "Overview" names, double highlight (longest match wins), add a Period accounting entry, breadcrumbs and a drawer below 1024 px. Audit §4 consolidation is not adopted.
- **Backend:** additive API changes are allowed (new optional fields, endpoints, query params); each is flagged and the OpenAPI v2 snapshot updated. No breaking changes.
- **Glossary:** **Stop** = downtime event, **Event** = the generic record; **Unaccounted** = reconciliation residual; **Investigation** = RCA case (module name "RCA" kept); **Action** = corrective action; US spelling ("organization"); "Whole plant" instead of "Tenant-wide".

Defaults applied until the SME says otherwise (audit §8):
- Corrective-action model (F-3): no model merge. Overview progress counts actions (the closure basis); recommendations/tasks show their relation; tasks link to `/rca/:id`.
- RCA link removal (G-3): not built (no unlink).
- New case priority: not derived from impact; user picks.
- Product name: UI keeps the branding app name ("Traverse Forge"), read from one place.
- Dark mode: out of scope.
- Event comments: out of scope.
- Per-event impact: show what the server returns, labelled approved/provisional as provided.

## 2026-10-18 — Status tones (UX audit C-1 / C-2 / C-5 / C-7)

Applied with the §5.8 rule (amber = data quality / provisional, red = overdue / above tolerance / failed, informational states never red). One `StatusBadge` (`src/features/ux-kit/status-badge.tsx`) with tones neutral / info / success / warning / danger; domain maps in `src/features/ux-kit/labels/status-tones.ts`.
- **Priority (tasks and RCA cases):** Critical = danger, High = warning, Medium = neutral, Low = neutral (Medium is not blue, so urgent rows stand out).
- **RCA case status:** Closed = success; In progress, Analysis, Published (older), Monitoring, Closure review = info; Draft = neutral.
- **Versions:** approved / published = success, submitted = info, draft / superseded / retired = neutral.
- **Residual "Above tolerance"** = danger (was amber); **tolerance budget** past review / mismatched / missing = danger.
- **Escalation** (My queue task, SLA badge) = info; only **Overdue** is danger. Waiting for data and repeated rejections = neutral.
- **Provisional FX rate**, RCA recommendations (provisional or not) = warning and info respectively, never danger.
- **Deactivated user / retired record or code** = neutral (a state, not an alarm).
- **Unit states** (both timelines, downtime trend chart): one `--state-*` token per state; downtime stops are coloured by schedule class (unplanned = stopped, planned = planned, external = starved amber), unknown/unclassified = pale fill + hatch.

## 2026-10-18 — Period accounting entry: which plan (UX audit B-5, default until the SME answers audit §8 Q7)

PLA → "Period accounting" (`/period-accounting`) resolves the plan only when opened: if exactly one plan has an approved current version valid now (`[validFrom, validTo)`) for the `?units=` unit (when exactly one is given) or the plant, it opens `/plans/:id`; with none or several (or more plans than one page of 200) it opens `/planning?tab=plans` with a toast hint. Gated like Plans (`canViewAnalysis`). The sidebar makes no request for it.

## 2026-10-18 — Event list duration, source-owned fields and filtered period grid (UX audit E-1 / E-5 / D-5 / D-6, Phase U3 backend)

- **Duration of a running event (E-1):** `sort=durationSeconds|-durationSeconds` orders by the stored `end − start`; an event without an end sorts as the **longest** (`COALESCE(duration_seconds, 'Infinity'::numeric)`, first with `-durationSeconds`, last with `durationSeconds`, ties by id). Not by elapsed time: that key moves while the stop runs, so keyset pages would skip or repeat rows. Index `IDX_production_events_tenant_duration` (migration `ProductionEventListIndexes`) serves the sort.
- **`minDurationSeconds` (E-1)** uses the duration the page shows: closed events by `end − start`, running events by the time elapsed until the request (`elapsedSeconds`), so a stop that has run 10 minutes does not pass a 1-hour filter.
- **`verificationStates` (E-7):** `verified` = `verified_at` set (SME D13 supervisor verification), `not_verified` = not set; the maintenance validation is not part of it.
- **`sourceOwnedFields` (E-5, single event reads):** manual events own nothing to a source (`[]`); any non-manual event owns its interval (`startTime`, `endTime`, `durationSeconds` — PATCH answers 409 SOURCE_OWNED); once a source revision exists, every overridable column of the revision model (`OVERRIDABLE_FIELDS` without raw attribute facts) is listed too — editing one is recorded as a source override. Derived from the constants the writes use, not a copy.
- **Bulk asset (A-1):** `codes.assetId` must be equipment of every selected event's unit (422 INVALID_REFERENCE with the offending event ids); like a single PATCH it is written through the user-change path, so on source events it becomes a recorded override.
- **Period grid residual filter (D-5 / D-6):** the residual state is domain arithmetic over saved decisions, not a column, so `residualStates` scans keyset batches of 500 and examines at most 2,000 periods per request; a filtered page can therefore hold fewer than `limit` rows while `nextCursor` is set — clients page until `nextCursor` is null. Each row carries `planId` (plan of the selected plan version), so one list spans every plan of the scope.


## 2026-10-18 — RCA additive API (UX remediation U3)

- `GET /v2/rca-actions` (mine/owner/status/overdue/rcaId/unit/due window, keyset by due date, undated last). Overdue = due date before `now()` and status open/in progress — the same rule as the RCA summary count, so counts match.
- `GET /v2/rcas` rows carry `trigger` (first linked event by link time) and `actions {total, done, overdue}`; `total` excludes cancelled actions. New `open` filter.
- Action `cancelReason` is optional (stored with `cancelledBy`/`cancelledAt`, cleared on reopen); the UI asks for it but the API does not require it, so older clients keep working.
- Evidence `taskAssigneeId`; `verifiedBy`/`verifiedAt` are recorded by the server when `verified` becomes true (client values ignored).
- `GET /v2/occurrences` must be bounded by a time window (≤ 366 days) or an event id.
- Event-diagram concurrency is a frontend fix: the save sends the row version the tool opened with; the server already answers 412 on a stale If-Match.
- Open question for the owner: `rca-case/approved-impact.ts` does not filter by ledger when falling back to an approved run's lines (older approvals), so it may add capacity-ledger lines to plan-ledger lines; the new attributed-loss endpoints use the plan ledger only. Unchanged until the owner decides.


## 2026-10-18 — Integrity and connection fixes (`plans/INTEGRITY_FIXES.md`)

Source: `docs/INDUSTRY_CAPABILITY_ASSESSMENT.md` §0 Q2. Owner: "first we need to fix these … rca should be on v2".

- **RCA is v2 for every plant (owner decision).** The v1 auto-create on event save (`rca/rca-auto-create.service.ts`, `rca-trigger-evaluator.ts`, reading `rca_trigger_configs`) is removed. The RCA trigger services and the 5-minute open-event sweep no longer read `v2.rca`. The flag stays in the registry with default `true` and **no server effect**, so cutover tooling and existing tenant rows stay valid. The RCA policy page no longer shows the "flag off" alert. The `rca_trigger_configs` table stays (no data dropped); its unused service, module and DTO files are left for a later cleanup.
- **Coding verification is a revision (item 1, engineering default).** Supervisor verification and maintenance failure validation each write one `event_revisions` row per event (kind `user`, `change_reason` `verification` / `failure_validation`; the revision-kind enum is unchanged, so no migration). They also write one `EventChanged` outbox row with `workflowOnly: true` in the same transaction, and `row_version` goes up by one. The period-recalc and stop-events consumers skip workflow-only messages, because a stamp changes no quantity, timing, coding or impact. The RCA trigger consumer still evaluates them, which is idempotent. A source refresh that moves an event's times still does not clear verification; that behaviour predates this change.
- **"Start RCA" where the loss is seen (item 4).** Loss candidates in the period workspace, PLA loss events, downtime Pareto reasons, downtime asset bad actors and the reliability bad-actor table open `/rca/new`, carrying the context in the URL:
  - `eventId`: the event flow.
  - `lossLineId`: a `loss_line` link is added after the case is created or chosen. If that link is refused, the page stays with the error and a link into the saved case.
  - `title` / `description` / `unitId`: prefill a standalone case.

  The button is shown only to RCA writers. Not-coded Pareto bars and asset-less groups get no button. The server still validates every id.
- **In-app reminders (item 6, engineering defaults; SME to confirm).** Six new notification kinds, evaluated by a worker job every 10 minutes:
  - `rca_action_due` / `rca_action_overdue`: go to the owner. They escalate to the case team lead after 7 days overdue.
  - `task_due` / `task_overdue`: go to the assignee. They escalate to the assigner after 7 days.
  - `uncoded_backlog`: closed unplanned or unknown-class stops still uncoded 4 h after they ended (level 2 at 24 h), per unit and production day. Goes to operator and production engineer.
  - `long_open_stop`: an open unplanned stop running 2 h (level 2 at 8 h). Goes to production engineer and manager approver.

  Thresholds are env settings: `NOTIFY_DUE_LEAD_DAYS`, `NOTIFY_OVERDUE_ESCALATE_DAYS`, `NOTIFY_UNCODED_AGE_HOURS`, `NOTIFY_UNCODED_ESCALATE_HOURS`, `NOTIFY_LONG_STOP_HOURS`, `NOTIFY_LONG_STOP_ESCALATE_HOURS`, `NOTIFY_LOOKBACK_DAYS` (62). Recipients must be active members of the plant, and nobody escalates to themselves. One notification is sent per user and key; keys include the due date, so a rescheduled item is reminded again. Only the highest level reached is sent, except that the owner's first overdue notice and the escalation both go out, because they reach different people. There is no e-mail (no delivery infrastructure). Migration `1794220300000-WorkReminderNotifications` adds the kinds and two partial indexes. One of them, `IDX_production_events_open_start`, also serves the RCA open-event sweep.
- **Coding rules on automatic events (item 8, engineering default).** Active `coding_default` rules now run when MQTT, PI and state-engine events (derived stops and slowdowns) are created. Precedence is stated value > reason-code default > rule > event-type default, and rules fill only open axes. Each filled axis records the `declarative_rule` basis with the rule id and version in `classification_basis`, never in `user_overrides`. For derived stops, the schedule class from the state mapping is an engine fact. For derived slowdowns, only the physical type and the measured impact are facts. Rules apply on create only, as reason-code defaults do. Rule-filled axes survive source revisions, and user overrides win. **Open questions:**
  1. A source revision that changes `eventType` re-derives schedule class and OEE component, which resets rule-filled axes and leaves the basis stale.
  2. A PATCH that sets a new reason code drops earlier rule entries from the basis, although the values stay.


## 2026-10-18 — UX remediation U3 closing decisions

- **RCA approved impact counts the plan ledger only** (owner: "standard and common practice"). For revisions approved before approved lines were stored, `rca-case/approved-impact.ts` falls back to the approved run's lines and now filters `ledger = 'plan'`: the capacity ledger is an alternative baseline of the same loss, never added on top. Matches the attributed-loss endpoints.
- **"For you" dashboard row** (§5.6): there is no supervisor role; `operator` and `production_engineer` get current stops + stops to code, `production_accountant` periods due, `reliability_engineer` recommended investigations + my overdue actions, `manager_approver` pending approvals. Counts are the first server page's row count with "+" when more pages exist (no client totals).
- **Event Hub URL keys:** `sort=newest|oldest|longest|shortest` (newest omitted), `minDuration=<seconds>`, `verified=verified|not_verified`; rolling windows stay relative (`window=24h`).
- **Period links from an event** open `?view=approved` for approved rows; Back on the period page returns to the event it came from.
- **RCA link pickers** default to ±15 days around the trigger event (first linked event by link time), else the last 30 days.
- **My work** (`/my-queue`): tabs Periods · RCA actions · Tasks; the route stays `/my-queue`.
- **RCA field history (item 2, engineering default).** Every RCA case command writes the append-only `audit_log` in the same transaction, through one recorder (`rca-case/rca-audit.ts`). This covers create, patch (team, evidence, logic tree, event diagram, recommendations), delete, logic-tree reset, transitions, analyses, attachments, links, actions (create, patch, verify, baseline correction), causes, and task completion flow-back.
  - Entity types: `rca_case`, `rca_action`, `rca_case_link`, `rca_analysis`, `rca_cause`.
  - Stored per row: before/after hashes, changed field names, and small before/after values (status, owner, due date, team lead, priority, target date, unit, kind, cause code, recommendation, lengths of the root-cause and statement texts). Large JSON is never copied. Null and undefined hash the same.
  - Read: `GET /v2/rcas/:id/history` (keyset, newest first, case read scope). Migration `1794220600000-RcaFieldHistory` adds a partial index.
  - Not audited: dismissing a trigger recommendation (not tied to a case) and the hourly verification sweep (a system job).
- **Recommendation → action → task (item 5, engineering default).** `rca_actions.recommendation_id` is optional and must name a recommendation of the same case (422 `INVALID_REFERENCE`). Recommendation items get a server UUID when sent without an id. The case view lists the actions implementing each recommendation.
  - `tasks.rca_action_id` is a foreign key (ON DELETE SET NULL). A second open task for one action is refused (409 `ACTION_TASK_EXISTS`, plus a partial unique index).
  - When a linked task is completed, the action records `task_completed_at` and the history shows it, but **the action's status never changes**: completing an action still needs its type, evidence and an independent verifier.
  - Known gaps: removing a recommendation leaves its actions' `recommendation_id` dangling; the v1 task routes have no flow-back.
  - Migration `1794220400000-RcaRecommendationActionTasks`.
- **Typed causes and the coded root-cause gate (item 7, engineering default; SME to confirm the taxonomy).** New table `rca_causes`: kind `direct` / `contributing` / `root`, an optional cause code from `rca_cause_codes` (RESTRICT), a statement, and supporting evidence and logic-tree node ids. Row-level security applies.
  - Endpoints: `GET/POST /v2/rcas/:id/causes`, `PATCH/DELETE /v2/rca-case-causes/:id`. The item route is not `/v2/rca-causes/:id`, because that path already holds the cause-library routes.
  - Causes are locked in Closure Review and Closed (422 `CAUSES_LOCKED`). A retired code cannot be newly assigned (422 `CAUSE_CODE_RETIRED`).
  - `rcas.coded_root_cause_required`: existing cases false, so they keep the free-text rule. Every case created after migration `1794220500000-RcaTypedCauses`, by any path, is true. It needs at least one `root` cause with a code to request closure (blocker `CODED_ROOT_CAUSE_MISSING`, readiness `closure.rootCauseRule`).
  - **Dependency:** each plant must maintain its RCA cause catalogue (Master data → RCA causes) before new cases can close.
  - New report `GET /v2/reports/rca-causes`: cause codes ranked by number of cases, with the approved loss of those cases (the existing case-outcome calculation, at most 50 codes). Ranking by loss re-orders only that top set.

## 2026-10-18 — Marun E2E downtime test: two state-engine fixes (engineering default)

- **Late-sample horizon of the state engine.** The intake accepts store-and-forward samples up to the connection's past limit (MQTT default 7 days), but the engine only reopened its window for late samples inside the 24 h lookback, so a STOP observed more than 24 h before it arrived was stored and never became a stop (found replaying 17 Oct on 18 Oct: the Shift A stop was 32 h old). Late samples now reopen the window back to `STATE_ENGINE_LATE_SAMPLE_HOURS` (default 168 h, never shorter than the lookback), bounded by the mapping start. The first run of a cursor or mapping version still uses `STATE_ENGINE_LOOKBACK_HOURS` (24). Approved history is still protected by the restatement controls downstream. Test: `test/integration/stop-events.int-spec.ts` (late sample 30 h old).
- **A coded state-engine stop stays `derived`.** The write model re-derived `source_kind` from the `source` label on every update, so coding (or a source revision of) a state-engine stop turned it into `source` and an import into `source`; the engine then no longer saw the stop as its own (`loadEngineOwned` filters `derived`), and the UI origin changed from "Derived" to "From a connected source". The stored kind is now kept unless the `source` itself changes. Migration `1794220900000-RestoreDerivedStopSourceKind` sets rows with business id `state:%` and source "State engine" back to `derived` (idempotent; `down` is a no-op). Imported rows that were flipped cannot be recognised and are not repaired. Tests: `src/production-event/event-write-model.spec.ts`, `test/integration/stop-events.int-spec.ts`.


## 2026-10-18 — Marun E2E production loss accounting test: fixes and findings (engineering default)

- **A manual loss line is in the unit of its slice.** Manual lines were saved and approved with `uom = null`, so the loss tree split the day into a "unit unknown" group (4 t) and a tonne group (46 t), and the Loss Breakdown page showed only the first. Draft lines now take the slice's unit (`sliceUomOf`, from the run's ledger totals); approval falls back to it for lines saved earlier. Approved rows are immutable: Shift A revision 1 keeps its null unit; revision 2 (the restatement) carries `t`. Test: `test/integration/approved-ledger.int-spec.ts`.
- **Change-proposal figures are shown.** The server sends the before/after snapshot as lists (`slices`, `ledgers`); the panel only read plain values and nested records, so "Before" and "After" were empty. Lists of records are now shown per slice ("Reconciliation · …") and per run ledger ("Plan ledger · …"). Test: `src/features/plan-dashboard/adjustment-model.test.ts`.
- **An export download is saved byte for byte.** The client decoded the CSV as text, which drops the UTF-8 BOM, so the saved file never matched the "File checksum (SHA-256)" shown next to it. `v2Request` takes `asFile: true` (body read as a Blob); only the export download uses it. Test: `src/api/v2/client.test.ts`.
- **The export panel offers the job of the requested format.** Clicking "PDF" after a CSV export relabelled the finished CSV job "Download PDF" (saved as `.pdf` with CSV content) until the new job id arrived. The job id and its format now change together, and a new request hides the previous job. Test: `src/features/plan-dashboard/workflow/export-panel.test.tsx`.
- **Marun seeder valuation policy (script only, live data unchanged).** The server seeds an unapproved valuation policy on first read (SME V1–V8 pilot: economic impact by default). `step-finance.ts` approved that seeded draft instead of creating the guide's version, so the plant's policy in force is **economic impact by default**, not contribution margin. Both views are stored for every line (Shift A: margin 5,100 USD, economic 4,960 USD), so no number is wrong; only the default view differs from the guide. The seeder now approves only a version whose `defaultBasis` is the guide's, and `verify-train` checks it. Correcting the live plant needs a new valuation policy version by Darya, approved by Kamran — it cannot cover 17 Oct without a backdate (calculated periods exist), so it is left for the owner.
- **Not changed, for the owner:** an operator holds the disposition-writer API role but has no route to the period workspace (`canViewAnalysis`), so Omid cannot record a disposition in the UI; the UI has no "correct this disposition" action (the late laboratory result was recorded through `POST /v2/lot-dispositions` with `supersedesId`); a month close has no "month has ended" check (only existing periods are counted); "Certify the day" acts on one click without a confirmation; percentages in the coding gate are truncated to one decimal (66.6 %) while the server message and the Downtime pages round (66.7 %).


## 2026-10-18 — Marun E2E root cause analysis test: fix and findings (engineering default)

- **The 5-Why checklist uses the policy's refused root-cause phrases.** With root cause "Operator error" the 5-Why tool ticked "System-level root cause written (done)" and the save went through, while the server's acceptance gate refused it (`ROOT_CAUSE_MISSING`, `FIVE_WHY_ROOT_CAUSE`). `GET /v2/rcas/:id/readiness` now returns `rules.fiveWhyUnsupportedRootCauses` (the `rca_policy` `fiveWhy.unsupportedRootCauses` in force, additive field; the readiness response has no OpenAPI schema, so the snapshot is unchanged), and the checklist normalizes the text the way the gate does. The server gate is unchanged. Tests: `src/features/rca/rca-workspace-model.test.ts`, `test/integration/rca-cases.int-spec.ts`.
- **Maryam stays an eligible closure approver.** Accepting a PLA change proposal is not participation in an RCA case: independence is judged from the case's creator, lead, members, closure requester and append-only investigator list only (CA1). Confirmed on the Marun case: readiness `approver.canApprove = true` for Maryam, and she closed it.
- **Not changed, for the owner / SME (reliability lead):**
  1. **Effectiveness exposure counts time without data as run time.** `effectiveness-exposure.ts` computes run seconds as the window minus recorded downtime, so time before commissioning or after the feed stops counts as running. The Marun baseline shows "2,159 run hours" over 90 days on a plant with about 25 h of measurements, and the post-action window "17.7 run hours" includes about 12 h after the feed ended. Guide answer 13 / D2: a missing reading cannot support a run. Proposed: clip exposure to known state coverage (unknown time excluded, disclosed), as a new query version (`rca-exposure-v3`) so frozen baselines keep theirs.
  2. **Action owners outside the RCA writer roles cannot see or update their actions.** The team and owner pickers offer every plant member, but an operator (Omid) gets "No access" on `/rca` and `/my-queue`, and a production engineer (Leila) sees her action read-only and cannot record progress or evidence (the API also refuses: `FORBIDDEN_ROLE`). Decide whether owners may update their own action (never mark it done) and whether operators get "My RCA actions".
  3. The verification window of an action can still be edited after the action is done (the acceptance criteria stay frozen). The Marun test used this to compress the 600 h window to the time available, and the history shows it; decide whether the window should lock at completion.
  4. The policy's minimum post-action exposure is 720 run hours; the guide's training exercise uses 600 comparable hours.


## 2026-10-18 — Marun E2E follow-up decisions (owner)

- **Valuation basis:** the guide's baseline is contribution margin. The valuation policy gets the same audited commissioning backdate as source authority (effective-from in the past only while no approved value of the plant overlaps the range — otherwise revaluation follows the normal path; reason required). A contribution-margin default policy is recorded effective from 17 Oct 2026; approved snapshots keep their history.
- **Month close:** deferred. Mehr 1405 (plant close calendar) ends 22 Oct 2026; close is tested after every period of the month is approved and every day certified. Reported as pending, not failed.

## 2026-10-18 — Valuation policy commissioning backdate: implementation (engineering default)

Implements "Marun E2E follow-up decisions (owner)" → Valuation basis.
- **A past `effectiveFrom` is a backdate.** `POST /v2/valuation-policies` keeps `effectiveFrom` required and adds optional `backdateReason` and `restateApproved`. A past instant needs a reason (422 `BACKDATE_REASON_REQUIRED`). A reason or `restateApproved` without a past instant is refused (422 `BACKDATE_REASON_WITHOUT_EFFECTIVE_FROM` / `RESTATE_WITHOUT_BACKDATE`). This tightens the contract: earlier, any past instant was accepted silently. The showcase seeders and the Marun seeder now send a reason.
- **History.** The instant must follow the latest approved version's effective-from (409 `BACKDATE_REWRITES_HISTORY`). This applies even on the restatement path.
- **Approved figures.** When a period of the plant with an approved revision overlaps `[effectiveFrom, now)`, the version is refused with 409 `BACKDATE_OVERLAPS_APPROVED_VALUE`, and `details.periods` names those periods. The only exception is `restateApproved: true`, the normal revaluation path the owner named. Approval queues the SME V7 recalculation. Each approved revision in range gets a change proposal; a manager accepts it, and the period is resubmitted and approved again. Approved snapshots are never rewritten. Periods without approved figures are revalued directly.
- **Approval checks again.** Both checks run again under the tenant's valuation-policy lock, with now = approval time.
- **Where it is stored.** The reason and the requested instant use the existing `requested_effective_from` / `backdate_reason` columns. `restate_approved` is new: migration `1794221300000-ValuationBackdateRestatement`, expand only, default false.
- **What the view shows.** The view adds `backdateReason` and `restateApproved`.
- **Default view after approval.** Reports that open on the policy default view read the newest approved version in force, so they switch at once: loss tree, loss value, bad actors and RCA impact. An approved revision's own reviewed valuation keeps the default view frozen in its snapshot until it is restated.


## 2026-10-18 — Marun incident: accidental coding of the Shift C stop (owner decision applied; findings for the owner)

- **Incident.** At 15:33:24Z a persona walkthrough of in-app notifications ran as Leila. It opened the "1 uncoded stop(s) waiting for a reason" reminder and coded the Shift C stop `3ea50fb2…` (17 Oct 02:00–02:30 local) as SPEED-LOSS, a performance reason, on a full stop. It ran outside this project's Claude sessions; the evidence is in `audit_logs`, `event_revisions` and `user_notifications`. The owner decision ("Shift C stop left uncoded", entry "Marun HDPE training plant") was broken. The change reached approved Shift C revision 2: the open `event_change` proposal was reused by the valuation-policy approval, relabelled `rate_change`, and accepted in the 2.y restatement.
- **Correction (done by role in the UI, `docs/MARUN_E2E_REPORT.md` 2.z).**
  - Leila cleared the reason: event revision 3.
  - Maryam accepted proposal `5a2dd993…` with the revert reason.
  - Sara submitted; Maryam was refused as the reopener; Reza approved Shift C revision 3 and re-certified 17 Oct (`3836c139…`).
  - Approved revision 3: availability 14 t with no reason, quality 1 t, good 209 t, residual 0.
  - Day: 621 / 51 / 0, USD 7,650 at contribution margin; Shift C USD 2,250.
  - Day coding gate: 1 uncoded of 2 stops, coded ratio 66.7 %.
  - Revisions 1 and 2 are kept unchanged.
- **Rule for test sessions on this plant (engineering default):** walkthroughs and notification checks must not save an Event Hub edit on the two 17 Oct stops. Opening a reminder is a read; coding is a write. A test that needs a coding round-trip uses its own manual event.
- **Open, for the owner:**
  1. The stop's OEE component is still "performance". Clearing the reason kept the SPEED-LOSS default as a user override, and the deployed Edit / code form has no OEE component field. Restore it with Event Hub → Bulk code (OEE component availability, with a reason) as Leila; the automated session was not allowed to run it. No PLA figure changes: the approved line kind is availability.
  2. Bug: clearing a reason code leaves `coding_state = coded` / status "Coded" (`advanceStates` only moves forward). Gates use `reason_code_id` and are right, but the uncoded reminder, the uncoded index and the Event Hub coding filter read `coding_state`.
  3. Event edits (Edit / code and `PATCH /v2/production-events/:id`) cannot record a change reason.
  4. A reused change proposal overwrites its first trigger, and its snapshot is quantity-only. A reason-code change is therefore invisible to the manager who accepts it.
  5. The restatement panel shows a reason-only change as two unlabelled "Loss line · availability" rows.

## 2026-10-18 — Marun E2E RCA follow-up fixes (engineering default; owner / SME to confirm where noted)

- **Effectiveness exposure is known running time (rca-exposure-v3, SME D2 / CA2 / CA3).** Unit run time of a baseline or verification window is now the time the state timeline of every case unit shows `running` or `reduced_rate`, minus recorded downtime (planned and unplanned, as before). Unknown time — no data, stale, bad quality, unmapped, before the unit's first state mapping, in the future — is never run time. The timeline is read per mapping version in slices of at most 7 days (`rca-case/state-exposure.loader.ts`, pure maths `domain/rca/state-exposure.ts`). Each window stores `knownSeconds`, `unknownSeconds` and `coverage` (known / window). New baselines freeze `queryVersion: 'rca-exposure-v3'`; baselines frozen before keep their v2 hours.
  - **Verdict:** a post-action window with coverage below 1 is `insufficient_evidence` ("state data covers X % of the window … a verdict needs complete coverage"), whatever its hours, because a repeat during a data gap would go unseen. Engineering default = complete coverage, consistent with the accounting coverage gate default (`requiredCoverageRatio` 1). **SME question:** a tolerance (e.g. a policy `minStateCoverage`) for short heartbeat gaps.
  - The baseline window reports its coverage but is not gated (it is often computed before the window starts, so part of it can be in the future); its rate is incidents per known run hour.
  - A unit without any state mapping has no known run time: its actions verify as insufficient evidence. The standby running-time basis (standby log, CA4) is unchanged and not gated.
  - Marun: the 90-day baseline drops from "2,159 run hours" to the ~24 h actually observed; the post-action window reports the ~12 h without data.
- **Action owners work their own actions (owner rule).** The owner of an action who is not an RCA writer (reliability engineer, manager approver) may read the investigation (reads were already open to plant members; the UI no longer blocks them) and, while the action is open or in progress: set its status to open / in progress and record completion evidence (reference and files). Enforced in `PATCH /v2/rca-actions/{id}` by the RequestContext user id (`rca-case/action-owner-access.ts`): other fields or other people's actions 403 `FORBIDDEN_ROLE`, `done` 403 `SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES` (CA4: a verifier marks it done), a done or cancelled action 409 `ACTION_FINISHED`. Cancelling stays with writers. The case file upload (`POST /v2/rcas/{id}/attachments`) is open to the owner of an open action of the case. UI: "RCA Analysis" and "My work" are in the operator menu; `/rca` shows an operator "My RCA actions"; My work hides Periods from operators; the case is read-only for them except their action's progress and evidence.
- **Verification method and named verifier (CA4).** Actions take optional `verificationMethod` (text ≤ 2000) and `verifierId` (a member of the plant, 422 `INVALID_REFERENCE` otherwise; never the owner, 422 `VERIFIER_IS_OWNER`). When a verifier is named, only that person marks the action done (403 `NOT_THE_VERIFIER`; SuperAdmin excepted). Migration `1794221460000-RcaActionVerificationPlan`.
- **Verification window locked once done.** Changing `verificationWindowStart/End` of a done action answers 409 `VERIFICATION_WINDOW_LOCKED` (reopen the action first; the history shows both moves). The view carries `verificationWindowLocked`; the edit form disables the dates with the reason.
- **Approved manual loss lines can be linked.** `GET /v2/loss-lines` also lists the manual lines of a period whose latest non-cancelled revision is approved (`approved_loss_lines` with source `line`), with `source: 'manual'` (run lines `source: 'run'`; additive field). The `loss_line` link target id of a manual line is the manual line's id (`approved_loss_lines.source_id`); `link-targets.ts` resolves it from its latest approval (period bounds stand in for the segment). The approved impact already counted approved lines by `source_id`. New index `IDX_approved_loss_lines_tenant_source`.
- **Recommendations leave "Open" at closure.** When closure is approved, a case recommendation whose implementing actions are all done (cancelled aside) becomes `Completed`; one whose actions were all cancelled becomes `Cancelled`; one without implementing actions keeps its status (no rule says it was carried out). Audited as a case update.
- **Exports.** Investigation JSON adds the actions (owner, verifier, method, evidence, window, baseline, verdict), typed causes (root marked), links, closure (residual risk, request, approval, frozen verdict), outcome and the plant time zone; dates stay ISO / UTC (the export convention). Recommendations CSV: due day in plant time with the zone in the header plus the ISO / UTC column. Evidence CSV adds the verifier and the task assignee.
- **RCA flows never write an event's coding.** Linking and closing move only the event's RCA state and pointer (`moveEventRcaState`); the evidence and similar-case reads write nothing. Covered by `test/integration/rca-action-owners.int-spec.ts`. The Shift C recode during the RCA E2E (15:33Z) was not made by an RCA flow found in the code; check the event's revision history (actor, change reason) for the Event Hub edit that made it.
- **RCA policy form edits the workflow rules.** Effectiveness (minimum run hours, baseline lookback, required reduction, review days, zero-repeat classes), FMEA (method, RPN threshold, severity review), 5-Why (guideline / minimum levels, evidence per level, refused root causes) and the methods / minimum team per consequence class are fields of the manager-approver form; the FMEA priority table is carried over unchanged. Server validation unchanged.
- **Knowledge base text search** already exists (`Search knowledge entries`, server `q`); no change.
- Smaller: the fishbone "Primary cause" checkbox starts unchecked; the outcome panel's realized figures use the plant number format (2 decimals).


## 2026-10-18 — Marun E2E downtime follow-ups (engineering default)

- Reliability shows mean active repair time (MTTR, from work-order evidence) and mean time to restore production (full outage) side by side. A row without repair evidence shows the reason in the MTTR column. The reliability report's MTTR note is sent only while some unit or asset has no MTTR (envelope content only, shape unchanged).
- The event form states the impact's stored basis (`impactBasis`): approved impact rule for the asset, standby takeover, stated by a person, or derived from the unit state / from the source. A source-owned impact names this basis in its Override hint instead of "From (source)". The OEE component is on the form (empty = the reason code's default; existing `oeeComponent` DTO field).
- Event summary: "Event severity" (the event's own severity) and "Failure severity" (SME D8, stops) are separate rows.
- "Source" means the data source (e.g. "State engine · Derived"; list column "Source"). "Origin" is reserved for the unit / asset that caused the stop. The work-order table links "Open the stop".

## 2026-10-18 — Marun E2E: clearing a stop's reason, change reasons (engineering default)

- **Clearing a reason uncodes the stop.** A PATCH (or any write through `prepareUpdate`) that sets `reasonCodeId` to null on an event that had one makes `coding_state` `uncoded` again (status no longer "Coded"), unless the investigation is pending. This also applies to a reviewed coding. Coding verification is reset, as for every coding change (SME D13). Earlier, `null ?? existing` kept the old reason in the state rule, and states only moved forward, so the uncoded backlog reminder, the uncoded index and the Event Hub filter missed the stop. Gates and KPIs count `reason_code_id` and were right.
- **A cleared reason gives back what its code filled.** The schedule class or OEE component goes back when its `classification_basis` is the cleared code (`code_rule` / `code_default`):
  - It returns to the source's fact from the latest source revision, else the event type's default.
  - Its basis entry is removed and its user override is released, so the source owns the field again.
  - These stay as they are: values a person chose (basis `user`), declarative rule defaults, and the impact code (never replaced, existing rule).
  - If the request states the axis, that value wins.
- **Change reason.** `PATCH /v2/production-events/:id` accepts an optional `changeReason` (≤ 1000, additive). It is stored as `change_reason` on the user revision and on its coding revision. The Edit / code form has an optional "Reason for the change" field. It is sent only with a real change: a reason alone writes no revision. The OpenAPI snapshot and `schema.d.ts` are regenerated.
- Tests: `src/production-event/event-write-model.spec.ts`, `test/integration/stop-events.int-spec.ts`, `src/features/event-hub/model/event-hub-model.test.ts`.

## 2026-10-18 — Marun E2E production loss accounting follow-ups (engineering default)

- **Day certification asks for confirmation.** "Certify the day" opens a confirmation. It repeats the server's day roll-up (good of target, loss, attributed, residual, shifts) and the day's stop-coding verdict. There is no comment field, because the certify DTO has none.
- **Percent display rounds like the server.** `formatRatioPercent` now rounds half away from zero to one decimal, as the gate's `Decimal.toFixed(1)` text and the Downtime pages do. So 0.6667 reads 66.7 % everywhere; before, it was truncated to 66.6 %. A trailing `.0` is dropped. This is display only, chosen for consistency over "truncation never overstates". Gates decide on the server's ratio, never on the text.
- **Correcting an approved disposition.** Disposition writers (`DISPOSITION_WRITERS`) get "Correct this disposition…" on an approved lot that is not superseded and has no pending correction.
  - It opens the record form at `…/dispositions/new?supersedes=<id>`, pre-filled with the lot, product and interval.
  - The product is fixed (server rule `INVALID_SUPERSEDE`).
  - Saving proposes a new disposition with `supersedesId`.
  - Only a `quality_approver` who is not the author decides it, as before.
- **Operators reach Actuals & quality (the UI now follows the API role set).** Operators hold the disposition-writer role (SME E3, guide "Omid or Leila"), so the UI follows the API instead of the write being removed.
  - New capability `canReachPeriodQuality` (analysis readers or disposition writers) gates `/plans/:planId/periods/:periodId/*` and the disposition form.

  - The button is now "Pause for late data…".
  - The line shows the server's data state: "Data complete" when every product's actuals are `complete`, otherwise their quality (e.g. "Data: partly covered").
  - The "Waiting for data" badge still marks an open hold.
- **The PDF export names its valuation basis.**
  - The cover lists "Loss value basis".
  - Each revision lists its basis with the valuation policy version, and "Exchange rates" (frozen in the approval snapshot, or none, plus the provisional note).
  - The running header adds "Loss values: (basis)", or "several bases (see each revision)".
  - The CSV already had `valuation_view`.
- **Actuals provenance groups its segments.** Segments are grouped per source and method. Each group shows:
  - the signal's name (`signal:<id>` becomes the unit's signal name, never the id) and the method;
  - the slice coverage, when the slice has a single source;
  - the segment count by quality, and the time span.

  The individual segments sit behind an expandable detail. Nothing is added up in the browser.
- **Valuation-input change proposals (coordinator item a).**
  - A recalculation queued by an approved FX rate, valuation policy or demand status now has the reason `(subject)_changed`. It opens a `valuation_change` proposal instead of a "rate change".
  - New enum value: migration `1794221550000-ValuationChangeTrigger`, expand only; `down` maps it back to `rate_change`.
  - The proposal's "before" adds `values`: the approved loss value in the policy's default view, per reporting currency. These are the approval snapshot's reconciled totals, summed over slices on the server. The panel shows them with the basis name.
  - The value after is calculated when the proposal is accepted (the new draft is revalued), and the panel says so. No preview valuation was added, because it would duplicate the approval valuation path.

  - For that person, Approve is disabled with "You reopened this revision: another manager approver must approve it".

  - The server rule is unchanged.
- **A reused change proposal keeps every cause (note D).**
  - `detail.triggers` lists each cause, oldest first, with its reason and time. A proposal from before this change starts with its stored trigger.
  - The panel reads, for example, "Event changed, then Valuation policy changed". The `trigger` column keeps the latest cause, as before.
  - The "after" snapshot adds `codingChanges`: the plan-ledger lines whose reason code the new inputs change, matched by auto key. The panel shows, for example, "Reason changed: SPEED-LOSS → none · availability loss line 14.00".

- The default valuation view of the period Value section is not changed (owner decision pending).

## 2026-10-18 — Month close: month key and month end (engineering default)

- **Month key.** `:month` is `YYYY-MM` in the plant's close calendar (`month_close.financialCalendar`), or calendar-qualified as `persian:YYYY-MM` / `gregorian:YYYY-MM`. Years at or above 1700 are Gregorian; years below are Solar Hijri.
  - A bare key whose year can only belong to the other calendar is refused with 422 `MONTH_KEY_AMBIGUOUS`. In the Marun E2E test, `2026-10` had been read as Persian year 2026 (Gregorian 2647).
  - An explicit key of the other calendar is refused with 422 `MONTH_CALENDAR_MISMATCH`.
  - In both cases `details.overlaps` names the plant months the requested month overlaps (`2026-10` → `1405-07`, `1405-08`).
  - Keys are refused, never converted: a Gregorian month does not coincide with a Solar Hijri month, and a close step must act on the month the caller meant.
