Re-rendering a job made before this change gives different PDF bytes because the layout code changed, not the pinned labels.
- **Dashboard and PLA:** the two availabilities carry scope captions (OEE component of the calculated periods vs. run time ÷ scheduled time from the stop records); "Stops to code" shows a count from the Event Hub To-code query ("20+" beyond one page); the OEE trend offers "View values"; the Reconciliation period list uses the shared no-plan wording with the Plans link.
- **Month close states its real blocker (Planning N1, Aban):** the banner and the "Close quantities…" reason come from the server's counts and policy. When only the month end is missing: "Prerequisites met — waiting for the month to end." / "Month not ended yet."; a month that has not begun: "This month has not started yet." `GET /v2/month-closes/:month` adds `prerequisites.monthStarted`. No prerequisite rule changed.
- **A shift is verified after its stops are coded (Planning N8):** "Verify this shift's stops" is disabled with "Code the stop first" / "Code the N uncoded stops first" while any stop is uncoded. Server unchanged on purpose: it never verifies an uncoded stop (skipped with `NOT_CODED`, SME D13), and a bulk verification never fails as a whole, so refusing the whole shift with 409 would reverse D13.
- **Correcting an approved period by month state:** in an open month the server refuses a proposed correction (409 MONTH_OPEN), so History offers "Reopen for revision…" instead of "Propose a correction…"; a closing or locked month's dialog names that state; only SuperAdmin sees the self-decision clause.
- **Period page wording:** one grid label ("<plan> · period accounting") for the grid h1 and the period page back link; "Open My work"; "Unaccounted figures are always published"; My work › Periods empty state without a table header. With `ledger=capacity` the reconciliation notes it stays on the plan ledger (the loss lines are plan-ledger lines; the heading is not renamed). The capacity ledger's "Actual (good)" is the server's good output; figures missing on both sides are left out. The Value section names a restated loss value (old → new, server figures) and links to Review & close. The day coding verdict looks like the shift card. Posting treatments are sentence case. The `/period-accounting` redirect hint uses the accessible Sonner toast (the generated Radix toast keeps its axe issues elsewhere until a shared toaster wrapper replaces it).
- **Unsaved-changes guard uses the app dialog (Planning N2, P-5):** in-app links, back / forward and code-driven Cancel ask through the shared `UnsavedChangesDialogHost` (ConfirmDialog "Leave this page?" · "Leave and discard" / "Stay on this page", mounted once in App); only tab close / reload keeps the native prompt (`beforeunload`). The app uses `BrowserRouter`, so react-router's `useBlocker` (data routers only) is not available; the hook holds the click / history step and navigates after the answer, and returns `confirmLeave()` for Cancel buttons. Invalid text typed in a decimal field counts as unsaved. Import rates and Propose an actuals adjustment are guarded.
- **Planning opens:** a published (or not editable) calendar version opens at `/planning/calendars/:id` (read-only, Back to calendars); `/edit` is only for a draft the user may edit, the wrong URL redirects. Operating modes read in words ("Normal"); the Actions column is hidden from users who cannot act; the plan header target shows its unit when every slice's approved profile agrees ("672.00 t"); empty slice weight / target cells say why; "Go to date" outside the plan says so ("… is outside this plan (…): showing it from the plan start").

## 2026-10-19 — QA re-test round 3: money on the server, roles, shell and master data (engineering under "QA re-test decisions")

From the "Re-test 19 Oct" sections of `docs/qa/QA_SHELL.md` and `QA_MASTERDATA.md`, and the money findings of `QA_RCA.md` (N1), `QA_PLA_ANALYTICS.md` (N1–N5) and `QA_DOWNTIME.md` (N1–N3).

- **Money is withheld on the server (supersedes "Server responses are unchanged" of the 19 Oct guide-roles entry).** One capability on both sides: `canViewMoney` = the caller holds a role other than `Operator` / `operator` (server `auth/money/money-access.ts` `MONEY_EXCLUDED_ROLES`; frontend `capabilities.ts`, pinned by `server-role-sets.test.ts`; SuperAdmin and system work without a request keep money). One global interceptor (`MoneyRedactionInterceptor`, registered next to the idempotency interceptor) applies it to every route for a caller without the capability:
  - **Money-only requests answer 403 `MONEY_NOT_VISIBLE`** (audited as an access denial): `GET /v2/reports/loss-value`, `GET /v2/reports/loss-tree` with any metric but `qty`, `GET /v2/reports/bad-actors?rankBy=value`, every route of FX rates, valuation policies, valuation rates and demand statuses (`@MoneyOnly()`), `POST /v2/exports` and `GET /v2/exports/:id/download` (both reports carry value columns).
  - **Every other response leaves its money out** (`withoutMoney`): a money row (an object with a `currency` and `amount` / `nativeAmount` / `value` / `delta`, or a change-set field with `format: 'money'`) is dropped, an array that held only money rows disappears, and the shared money keys go wherever they appear (`claimedAvoidedLoss`, `claimedCurrency`, `claimedAvoided`, `estimatedCost`, `valuation`, `valuationTotals`, `approvedValuation`, `valueAfter`, `valuationView`, `nativeAmount`, `fxRateIds`, `fxProvisional`, `costThreshold`, `costCurrency`, `costPerHour`). Route-specific money keys with generic names are declared with `@MoneyFields(...)`: `cost` on the event routes (v2, v1 and job results), `values` on restatement and change-proposal routes. Quantities, durations, counts and states stay. "Omitted" means the key is absent (not null, which means "not valued"); the frontend types mark these fields optional.
  - **Exports:** the event CSV keeps its `cost` column in place, empty for such a caller; the RCA Actions / Recommendations CSV leave out the claim and estimated-cost columns (`CsvOptions.money`); the RCA case JSON is built from the redacted reads.
  - **UI:** the PLA tab bar lists Loss valuation only with `canViewMoney` (`plaPagesFor`); the Dashboard "OEE and downtime report" link follows the Reports gate; downtime "Lost value" toggle and "Export period summary"; RCA case header approved loss, Outcome "Claimed avoided loss", action "claimed …", recommendations "Est. cost"; the attributed-losses "Value basis"; impact-code valuation link and cost per hour; Bad actors has no in-page currency toggle (the header's is the only one).
  - Not covered: Socket.IO pushes (they carry no valuation today); writes are not refused (an operator cannot write any money field through the UI).
- **User page and Permissions:** the subtitle and the Permissions list show plant roles; the legacy role is "Sign-in type" (administrators only, hidden on a read-only profile), with help text stating the D06 fallback; module names on Permissions are the sidebar names (`sidebarNameOf`).
- **Approval delegations entry:** "User management" (users, permissions, delegations) is for administrators only (SuperAdmin, OrgAdmin, TenantAdmin). Approval-role holders who are not administrators (manager approver, production accountant, finance, quality and metrology approvers) record their own delegations under "Approval delegations" (`/delegations`, SME R1 / R6); `/users` sends them there and `/delegations` sends administrators to User management › Delegations.
- **Admin panel and Audit log for manager approvers stay:** manager approvers write the plant's display settings (server `canWriteSetting`; the Admin panel shows only Plant settings) and are audit readers (`AUDIT_READERS`).
- **Pending approvals for deciders only:** listed for the roles that decide some inbox kind (`APPROVAL_DECIDERS`, pinned to the server workflows). Operators keep it because they decide duplicate-stop merges; finance maintainers, master-data and integration administrators and viewers do not see it.
- **Equipment setup guide is a configuration journey:** listed for analysis readers and master-data administrators (`canReachSetupGuide`), not for operators.
- **Stale work reminders resolve:** the shared resolver marks `superseded` the open role-addressed reminders (uncoded backlog, long open stop, SLA checker reminders, SLA escalations) of a recipient who no longer holds the role on the unit (revoked grant, legacy fallback switched off by an own grant, ended delegation, deactivated account); other holders' copies stay; the reminder reopens if the role returns; person-addressed reminders are not affected. One-off: `scripts/marun-train/resolve-stale-reminders.ts`.
- **No approval-waiting bell notification for finance postings:** no notification kind announces a waiting approval for any workflow (only SLA-overdue period reminders), and postings have no SLA; waiting postings show on the Pending approvals tile and inbox. A posting reminder needs a posting SLA (open product decision).
- **Small shell wording:** "Availability loss line · <reason>" in approval change sets; the approval Request section in sentence case; a Dashboard KPI without product slices reads "No approved plan covers these periods"; the header shows the user's name and roles from 1024 px up (the icon's tooltip below), so the plant scope stays readable at 768 px.
- **Master data:** rule-builder errors are plain instructions per row ("Choose a field.", "Choose an operator.", "Enter a value."), never allowed-value lists or keys, and an untouched row stays quiet until Save. Date-only readiness and unit-state coverage windows are whole production days from the default site's operational day start (06:00), named by their start date (17 Oct = 17 Oct 06:00 → 18 Oct 06:00). Link texts and setup-step names come from `pageNameOf` / `sectionNameOf`. The SuperAdmin self-approval sentence is shown to SuperAdmin only. Tab bars list every page the user can open; coded records read "CODE — Name" in pickers; the derived MQTT client id is not shown.
- **Finance descriptive text can be corrected in place:** finance maintainers correct the FX source, demand evidence and policy notes / backdate reason through text-only routes (`PATCH /v2/fx-rates/:id/source`, `/v2/demand-statuses/:id/evidence`, `/v2/valuation-policies/:id/text`; additive, audited, figures, state and approval unchanged, nothing revalued) — a new version would backdate and revalue approved periods. Course wording ("training", "guide lesson 21") is removed from the seeder catalogue and from live data by `scripts/marun-train/rewrite-finance-texts.ts`.
- **Reliability engineers read the failure dictionary in the UI** (page and tab admit `canValidateFailures`; write controls stay with master-data administrators). The server write rule is unchanged for now (reliability engineers and manager approvers may still import through the API, which both seeders use, SME D11) — open item if writes must be master-data only on the server too.

## 2026-10-19 — PI Web API integration audit and sample PI (owner: "we are going with PI Web API; fix bugs and UI flaws, flag design items"; Basic authentication confirmed)

Audit of the PI path end to end against a sample PI Web API shaped like a real one (`npm run pi:sample`, `docs/PI_SAMPLE_SERVER.md`), run on the HDPE plant. Analysis: `docs/INTEGRATION_ANALYSIS.md`.

- **Fixed (engineering):**
  - **Primary element of a real PI frame.** A real PI Web API names the primary element only in `Links.PrimaryReferencedElement` and `RefElementWebIds`; it is now read from there (a single referenced element counts as primary, several never guess), at read and at ingest, so replays of retained frames place the same way (`integration/pi/pi-frame-shape.ts`).
  - **Element path kept.** The path looked up by WebId is written onto the frame, so `exact_path` / `pattern` rules match and the discovery sample shows the path and offers "Bind element".
  - **Delivered duration unit wins** over the rule unit (`{Value: 300, UnitsAbbreviation: 's'}` is 300 s under a minutes rule).
  - **Connection-specific attribute overrides beat newer "every connection" versions.**
  - **Deactivated connections run no jobs** (claim skips them; job creation answers 422 `CONNECTION_INACTIVE`).
  - **Test persists detected capabilities** (`batch`), so reads skip a missing batch controller from the first job.
  - **Credentials are refused at any depth** of `endpointConfig` (MQTT value-map tokens excepted, they are source data).
  - **401 explains itself** when the server offers no Basic (Negotiate/NTLM only).
  - **`streamsets/recorded` asks for at most 40 streams per call.**
  - **PI raises `unknown_code`** for a reason token the code library lacks, like MQTT (shared `unresolvedReason`; the MQTT `"Reason Code"` attribute name is now read for the delivered token).
  - **Period replays skip records closed as irrecoverable** ("never replayed"); naming the object or its exception still replays it.
  - **Index `(tenant_id, event_id)`** on `production_events` for ingest lookups (migration `ProductionEventSourceIdIndex`).
  - **Console:** the read scope (database/element, template) is in the URL and can be chosen on the Jobs tab; a scope-type choice (element scope was impossible) inferred from the WebId marker; job kinds for open-frame refresh, daily correction rescan and daily deletion check; upkeep jobs named, cadence "daily"; sample table shows the frame name; "Hours back" validated; bind dialog can include child elements (pattern `<path>\*`); quarantine list and page show the record, element path and delivered value; the unknown-code advice states that codes of an existing event are not changed by a replay (codes stay create-only, 2026-10-08); the stage tracker says "20+"; a signal backfill ending today is read up to now.
- **Open for the owner (recommendations):**
  1. **Event identity per source.** Existing events are matched on `(tenant, event_id)` only, so a PI WebId and an MQTT `event_id` (or two PI connections) can collide. Recommend scoping the lookup by source system and connection.
  2. **Source mapping history.** Rules are edited in place (`version` counts edits), so a replay of old data uses today's rule. Recommend append-only rule versions with effective dates.
  3. **Remapped frames do not move.** A source revision never changes `unit_id` (and codes stay create-only). Recommend: move the event when the placement changes and the event is not yet approved.
  4. **Hierarchy fallback for paths.** A frame on a child element is placed only by an explicit pattern (now offered in the bind dialog). Recommend an optional "ancestor path" tier after `pattern`.
  5. **AF browsing.** Discovery stops at databases; elements, attributes and stream WebIds (for signals) are typed by hand. Recommend element / attribute browse endpoints.
  6. **Code resolution in the mapping preview.** The preview does not say whether a reason token resolves to a library code; unknown codes are first seen after ingestion.
  7. **Child event frames.** `searchFullHierarchy` is not sent; nested frames are not read. Confirm whether the plant uses child frames.
  8. **Security defaults.** The egress allowlist is open when `CONNECTOR_EGRESS_ALLOWLIST` is empty and credential encryption falls back to a built-in key without `CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY`/`JWT_SECRET`. Recommend refusing to start in production without both.
  9. **Per-record mapping loads.** The normaliser loads the attribute profile, resolver and coding rules for every frame; ancestors are looked up one GET at a time. Recommend a short per-connection cache keyed by mapping versions.
  10. **Diagnostics are browser-session only.** The stage tracker's "Validate" and "Discover" reset on reload. Recommend storing the last test result on the connection.


## 2026-10-19 — Tier 1 / Tier 2 decisions (`plans/TIER1_TIER2_PLAN.md`)

- **D-T1 Rate kinds (owner: "according to our guide and standards").** Meanings follow `docs/PROACT_APPLICATION_GUIDE.md` and SME answer Q16 (`plans/SME_ANSWERS_ALL_PHASES_2026-10-14.md`, GE PLA benchmark):
  - **nameplate:** the design reference;
  - **demonstrated:** a rate the unit has proven it can reach;
  - **sustained:** the approved sustainable capability, used by the capacity ledger;
  - **planning:** the time-specific planned rate, used by the plan ledger;
  - **budget / forecast:** imported reference rates.

  The capacity-gap report shows them side by side, never adds the views together, and never lowers capability from recent performance.
- **D-T2 v1 cutover (owner: "yes").** The v1 → v2 cutover is complete, so v1 routes with no remaining consumer may be retired (410 Gone) or scoped and paginated.
- **D-T3 Recurrence watch (owner: agreed).** A closed case is watched for 180 days. A same-mode unplanned incident rate above the case's frozen baseline recommends "re-evaluate". The case is never reopened automatically.
- **D-T4 Investigation depth (owner: "standard and common practice").** API RP 585 / CCPS-style tiers by consequence class:
  - **minor:** 5-Why;
  - **moderate:** 5-Why or fishbone, plus a team charter;
  - **major:** logic tree or fault tree, plus a team charter and coded causes;
  - **critical:** logic tree plus FMEA or fault tree, a team charter, coded causes and independent closure (already required).

  To be confirmed against the plant's own matrix when it is available.
- **D-T5 Weighted multi-reason stops (owner: "standard and common practice").** As in Yokogawa Exaquantum/DTA:
  - Weights are optional, set by the person who codes the stop, and must sum to 100%. They can be entered as percent or duration.
  - A stop crossing a day or shift boundary is split pro-rata.
  - Weights split one stop's own loss only.
  - Concurrent separate stops still follow the overlap policy (`first_out`), and the weighting applies inside the share the stop received.
  - Conservation checks are unchanged.

## 2026-10-19 — Tier 1 implementation (engineering defaults; `plans/TIER1_TIER2_PLAN.md`)

- **1.1 Recurring causes page** at `/reliability/causes` (menu "Recurring causes", same gate as Bad actors). It shows the server ranking from `GET /v2/reports/rca-causes`; nothing is summed or re-sorted in the browser. Roles without money access get no loss column and rank by number of investigations only. The drill goes to `/rca?causeCodeId=`. **API (additive):** `GET /v2/rcas?causeCodeId=<uuid>` lists the cases that have a typed cause with that catalogue code, retired codes included, within the caller's scope.
- **1.2 Capacity gap report.** **API (new):** `GET /v2/reports/capacity-gap?at&unitIds&productIds&limit`. It returns one row per approved profile in force at `at`, across all operating modes, at most 200 rows with a `truncated` flag. The approved rate in force is shown for each kind.
  - Signed gaps N−D, D−S and S−P, each with ratio gap ÷ upper. Ratios S÷N and P÷S.
  - A gap or ratio is null with a reason (`not_set`, `uom_mismatch`, `timebase_mismatch`, `zero_base`). Units are never converted.
  - Budget and forecast are shown for reference only.
  - The page sits under PLA analytics → "Capacity gap". "As of" means 00:00 plant time on the chosen date.
- **1.4 Rule provenance on PATCH.** Setting a new reason code keeps the `declarative_rule` basis of axes the request doesn't write (item 8, open question 2, closed).
- **1.6 v1 surfaces (D-T2).** Seeders, e2e specs and integration fixtures still use these routes, so they are **hardened, not retired. Public API changes:**
  - v1 `GET /api/production-events` always returns `{items,total,page,pageSize}`: default 50 rows, max 200, and `pageSize` > 200 is a 400. The unpaged array is gone.
  - v1 `GET /api/tasks` without `tenantId` returns the acting tenant's tasks only.
  - v1 `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/tasks/:id` return 404 outside the acting tenant, including for SuperAdmin.

  `unmapped_events` is no longer written: intake `ingestion_exceptions` is the only quarantine. The table is kept.

  **Still open:** the legacy `/socket.io` gateway has no client but is injected by about 16 v1 services, so retiring it is a separate step. v1 `GET /api/production-events/:id` still relies on RLS for scope.
- **1.5 Orphan v1 RCA tables.** Twelve entity-only folders (`fishbone-diagram` … `rca-recommendation`) are kept. Removing them requires a drop migration of tables that may hold old v1 data, so they wait for an owner decision.
- **1.3 SuperAdmin self-decisions are visible.** One server-computed field, `selfDecided`.
  - Source: the stored `super_admin_override` on adjustments, dispositions and uncertainty budgets. Everywhere else it is derived from actor ids, because a match can only be a SuperAdmin; everyone else gets a 403.
  - Views that carry it: master-data and plan versions, FX, valuation policy, demand status, source authority and status mappings, the reconciliation revision and its approval history (approve, reject and bridge review, each by its own command's independence rule), change proposals, day certification, month-close history steps, RCA closure, and action completion. Baseline corrections carry `baselineCorrectionSelfDecided`.
  - Approved-ledger CSV gains a last column `self_approved`. The PDF gains the fact line "Approval: Self-approved (SuperAdmin)".
  - Badges: "Self-approved (SuperAdmin)" for approvals, "Self-decided (SuperAdmin)" for rejections, reviews, sign-offs and certifications, in warning tone.
  - Not exposed yet: coding verification/validation (needs a per-event lookup on the event list), tolerance adoption, restatement posting, plan "return", rule activation (the author is not kept after activation). No new durable flag was added.
  - Month-close marks are derived from the history window the view reads.

## 2026-10-20 — Tier 2 implementation (engineering defaults on D-T3 / D-T4 / D-T5; `plans/TIER1_TIER2_PLAN.md`)

- **2.1 Loss → Cause → Action → Outcome portfolio.** **API (new):** `GET /v2/reports/rca-portfolio` (`from`/`to` ≤ 400 days, `unitIds`, `causeKind` root|direct|contributing, `currency` USD|IRR, keyset `limit` ≤ 50). Page "Cause portfolio" at `/reliability/portfolio`.
  - A case counts when it was **opened** in the window.
  - Rows are per catalogue code of the chosen cause kind, plus an "Uncoded" row. A case counts once in every code it names, so rows are never totalled.
  - Approved loss uses the same calculation as the case outcome and the recurring-causes report.
  - Approved, claimed and realised figures are separate and never summed. Other-currency claims are counted, not converted.
  - Realised difference is summed per metric over each case's latest evaluated action.
  - Follow-up: a grouped variant of the approved-lines query, so approved impact is one statement per page instead of one per row.
- **2.2 Recurrence watch (D-T3).**
  - Baseline: the highest pre-action rate among the case's verified actions, with the exposure basis frozen with it. Cases without a verified action are not watched.
  - Window: closure to now, at most 180 days.
  - It fires when at least one same-mode incident occurs and incidents ÷ max(run hours, the action's minimum exposure, default 720 h) × 1,000 exceeds the baseline.
  - The result is a `reevaluate` recommendation (fired after closure). A manager approver dismisses it, or reopening accepts it; the case is never reopened automatically.
  - Migration `1794240200000-RcaRecurrenceWatch`. `GET /v2/rca-recommendations` gains `trigger` and `rcaId` filters.
- **2.7 Investigation depth (D-T4).**
  - New consequence class **`critical`**: migration `1794240210000`, and the `consequenceClass` enum gains `critical`. `significant` serves as the "moderate" tier.
  - The matrix `d-t4-v1` is a code constant applied on top of the plant's `rca_policy` tiers. A deeper method satisfies a lower tier (API RP 585 "minimum rigour").
  - Unclassified cases still get `CONSEQUENCE_CLASS_MISSING`. Cases already in Monitoring or later are never blocked.
  - Readiness codes: `METHOD_REQUIRED` (with `methods`), `TEAM_CHARTER_REQUIRED`, `CODED_CAUSE_REQUIRED`; `rules.investigationDepth` describes the rule in force.
  - Follow-up: the RCA policy editor does not show the `critical` tier yet.
- **2.3 Calendar-basis downtime KPIs.**
  - A calendar day is a plant production day: the default site's timezone and operational day start, 23 or 25 hours on a DST change, UTC midnight when there is no site.
  - It is used only where no calculated period overlaps the unit and day. A partly calculated day stays run-based, so nothing is mixed.
  - The response carries a separate `calendarBasis` section (calendar availability, total and unplanned downtime, stop count, MTBF on confirmed failures over calendar run time), never added into run-based totals.
  - It is left out (with a reason in `omitted`) under approved-only, product, shift or crew, or schedule-class filters.
  - The micro-stop and overlap rules come from the unit's `oee_policy`, or the D04 default, marked `policy: 'default'`.
- **2.4 Accounting exceptions queue.** **API (new):** `GET /v2/reconciliation/exceptions` (operational dates ≤ 400 days, `types`, `unitIds`, `workflowStates`, keyset). A new "Exceptions" tab in Month close.
  - It covers periods that are not approved, with eight types, read from stored state only.
  - Residual, claim, bridge and plan-allocation conditions reflect the latest calculation, not unsaved draft decisions.
- **2.5 RCA case PDF.** **API:** `POST /v2/exports` gains `reportType: rca_case`, `revisionSelector: case` and `rcaId` (PDF only, money roles only, 404 for a case the caller cannot read). Migration `1794240100000-ExportRcaCase`; its down leaves the enum values in place.
  - The case is pinned at request time, so re-rendering gives the same bytes.
  - Money appears in the display currency only.
  - The timeline comes from the case history.
  - Self-decided closure and actions are marked.
- **2.6 Rules on source revisions.** When a source revision changes the event type, axes stated by a user or a reason code keep their value. Every other axis is re-filled by the active `coding_default` rules, with the `declarative_rule` basis, or else takes the event-type default. Rules are loaded only when the type actually changes (item 8, open question 1, closed).
- **2.8 Weighted multi-reason stops (D-T5).** **API:** `PATCH /v2/production-events/{id}` and bulk-code `codes` gain `reasonWeights` (percent, or seconds for closed stops; at most 10 reasons). The event view gains `reasonWeights`. Migration `1794240400000-WeightedReasonStops`.
  - Weights are stored to 8 places with the remainder on the largest. The primary reason is the largest weight (ties go to the one the request names).
  - Setting a new reason code or clearing the reason removes the split. A changed split clears verification.
  - In the engine, after `first_out` / `equal_share` allocation, a stop's segment share is split by weight into one line per (event, reason), with evidence share = segment share × weight. Conservation is unchanged, and unweighted results are byte-identical (fixtures WR01–WR04).
  - Weights apply to stops and micro-stops, not to fractional derates or grade transitions. Upstream starvation inherits the upstream stop's weights.
  - **`ACCOUNTING_ENGINE_VERSION` 2.8.0** and **`KPI_ENGINE_VERSION` 2.7.11**: the downtime breakdown by reason splits weighted stops by weight, and the stop count stays under the primary reason.
  - Follow-ups:
    - Bulk-code dialog UI has no split section yet.
    - Merge takes the template event's split.

## 2026-10-20 — v1 RCA tables dropped; Tier 2 follow-ups

- **v1 RCA tables dropped (owner: "yes drop v1").** Migration `1794240500000-DropV1RcaTables` drops the ten v1 RCA tables that nothing reads or writes any more: `fishbone_diagrams`, `five_why_analyses`, `fault_tree_analyses`, `fmea_analyses`, `barrier_analyses`, `change_analyses`, `logic_tree_nodes`, `rca_analysis_configs`, `rca_evidence`, `rca_recommendations`.
  - All ten were empty on the plant database, and no other table referenced them.
  - The entity folders are removed.
  - `down()` recreates the empty tables exactly as before (from `src/database/legacy/v1-rca-tables.ddl.ts`): keys, indexes, foreign keys, child-tenant triggers and RLS.
  - `evidence_categories` and `evidence_locations` are **kept**, because the v2 evidence options read them.
  - Still open: the dead v1 columns on `rcas` (`calculated_roi`, `effectiveness_score`, …) and the legacy PLA tables.
- **Tier 2 follow-ups fixed.**
  1. On the Capacity gap view the header reads "<units> · As of <plant date | now>" instead of the shared window.
  2. The bulk-code dialog offers "Split across several reasons". It is percent only: it sends `codes.reasonWeights`, the largest share becomes the reason, and Preview stays blocked until every row has a reason and a percent.
  3. The RCA policy editor takes its classes from `RCA_CONSEQUENCE_CLASSES`, so it shows and saves the `critical` investigation tier and the zero-repeat class. Older versions without `critical` stay valid ("not set — the default applies").

## 2026-10-20 — Database audit (`docs/db-audit/AUDIT_REPORT.md`)

- **Integration tests on their own server (F05, owner: "yes").**
  - `npm run test:int` starts the throwaway in-memory container `proact-test-postgres` (`docker-compose.testdb.yml`, `127.0.0.1:5434`, fsync off) and points the suites at it through `TEST_DB_*`.
  - `npm run testdb:down` removes it. GitHub Actions runs `test:int:ci` on its own service container.
  - Why: schema-recreating test runs on `proact-postgres` caused checkpoints syncing 10k–23k files (up to 82 s fsync) and 0.6–2 s commits in the app.
  - `proact` stays the only database on `proact-postgres`.
- **Server settings (F08 / F14, owner: "yes").**
  - `pg_stat_statements` is preloaded (migration `1794240700000-PgStatStatements`).
  - Logging: `track_io_timing`, `log_min_duration_statement` 500 ms, `log_lock_waits`, `log_temp_files` 0, `log_autovacuum_min_duration` 1 s.
  - Planner and memory: `shared_buffers` 512 MB, `random_page_cost` 1.1, `effective_io_concurrency` 200, `jit` off.
  - `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` 15 min. It is a backstop, not 60 s, because the outbox relay transaction can legitimately sit idle while consumers run (F03).
  - Applied to `proact-postgres` with `ALTER SYSTEM` (undo with `ALTER SYSTEM RESET <name>` + restart), and to the bundled `docker-compose.yml` service via `command:`.
- **Retention of superseded calculation runs (F01).** Engineering default chosen under standard practice: immutable *posted* records are kept forever, *draft* working state is pruned, and runs are reproducible from their frozen fingerprint. Owner asked: "which suits here according to standard".
  - **Kept forever:**
    - every `calculation_runs` header (small; it holds the fingerprint, input hash, engine and policy versions, so any run can be explained and re-run);
    - the full detail (`kpi_results`, `loss_lines`, `loss_line_valuations`, `loss_line_evidence`) of every run that is or was a revision's current run when it was submitted, approved or published, or that an approval snapshot or export references;
    - approved ledgers, as already decided.
  - **Pruned:** the detail rows of `superseded` runs of draft revisions that were never submitted, once older than **30 days** (same window as the audit records, `CALC_RUN_DETAIL_RETENTION_DAYS`, unset = keep). The daily retention job does the pruning in small batches.
    - The immutability triggers stay. As for `audit_log`, they allow DELETE only inside a transaction that declares a cutoff (`app.calc_retention_cutoff`) and only for rows of superseded, never-submitted runs older than it. UPDATE is still refused.
  - **At the source:**
    - Recompute of an **open** period (its open events count to "now", so every change makes a new input hash) is coalesced to at most one run per period per `OPEN_PERIOD_RECOMPUTE_MIN_SECONDS` (default 300 s).
    - Closed periods and explicit user actions (revise, settle, approve) still recompute immediately.
  - Measured on `proact` (20 Oct): 326 runs, 16 current, 25,108 of 25,976 `kpi_results` rows on superseded runs, and about 20 runs an hour for one open period. Expected effect: roughly 30× fewer detail rows, with history bounded by 30 days of draft churn.
  - **Status:** decided, not yet implemented. Needs a migration (trigger functions), the retention job, the recompute coalescing, and tests.

## 2026-10-20 — All unused v1 tables dropped; cutover tool retired (owner: "check all other v1 tables … if not drop them all", option "A + B + C")

- **How it was checked.**
  - Every TypeORM entity (173 tables) was matched to its references, using the class and the table name, across live backend code, the cutover tool, scripts and tests, and the frontend.
  - Name clashes were checked through imports: `ProductionLoss` is also an event type, and `DataSource` is also TypeORM's class.
  - Row counts and incoming foreign keys were read on `proact`. No views or functions depended on the tables.
- **Dropped:** migration `1794240600000-DropV1LegacyTables`, 25 tables. `down()` recreates them empty from `src/database/legacy/v1-legacy-tables.ddl.ts`.
  - **v1 tables nothing used:** `stream_alerts`, `stream_connection_configs`, `stream_data_sinks`, `stream_data_sources`, `stream_metrics`, `stream_processors`, `query_history`, `historian_tags`, `pi_af_elements`, `pi_events`, `data_sources`, `production_plan_templates`.
  - **v1 tables only the cutover tool read:** `production_losses`, `reconcile_forms`, `loss_categories`, `oee_summaries`, `rca_trigger_configs`, `unmapped_events`. The 3 superseded quarantine rows were dropped; `ingestion_exceptions` is the quarantine.
  - **The cutover tool's own tables:** `legacy_id_crosswalk`, `legacy_reported_values`, `legacy_write_freezes`, `migration_changes`, `migration_explanations`, `migration_reports` (1 cutover report row dropped), `migration_review`.
- **Removed code:**
  - `src/migration-cutover/**`: the comparison, remediation, report, CLI helpers, and the global v1 write-freeze interceptor.
  - `scripts/cutover/*`.
  - The 13 entity folders.
  - The cutover integration specs and their fixture.
  - The frontend message for `LEGACY_WRITES_FROZEN`.
  - The shared CLI argument parser moved to `src/common/cli/cli-args.ts`, together with its own `UUID_PATTERN`, because seeders and maintenance scripts still use it.
- **Kept, because v2 still uses them:** every other v1-era table, including `units`, `assets`, `products`, `production_events`, `production_plans`, `tasks`, `pi_connections`, `pi_mappings`, `pi_sync_jobs`, `pi_sync_watermarks`, `af_bindings`, `verification_status_codes`, `evidence_categories`, `evidence_locations` and `app_settings`.
- **Coordination:** another session's `1794240610000-DropPiAfElementDuplicateIndex` now skips when `pi_af_elements` is absent. Its `1794240600000-MeasurementSamplesInsertedIndex` shares this migration's timestamp; the two are independent, so order doesn't matter.

## 2026-10-20 — Operating reports R1–R12 (owner: "approved go ahead"; plan `plans/REPORTS_R1_R12.md`)
Engineering defaults; change any of them on request.
- **R1 month to date** runs over the financial month of the month-close policy (Gregorian or Persian), in whole production days.
- **R1 off-grade** is downgrade + transition off-grade + scrap + rework + not-yet-classified rejects, summed on the server. Wide spec is shown separately because it is conforming output (SME E3).
- **R2 handover notes** are stored in `shift_notes` and are append-only: a correction is a new note, and notes are never edited or deleted. They are keyed by unit, plant date and shift label. Any role that records events (`EVENT_WRITERS`) can write one; anyone who can read the unit can read them.
- **R2 shift window:** the stored shift instances of the chosen units, from the earliest start to the latest end. The events listed are the ones overlapping the window, including those still running, capped at 100.
- **R3 statement** covers one financial month of the month-close policy: its production days `[firstDate, endDate)` at the plant's day start. It reads approved figures by default, as the Reports page does. Its PDF is the designed report export (see Report exports); the print styles stay, so the browser's print is still clean. Restatements listed are approved revisions that replaced an approved one, for periods of the month, newest first, capped at 200.
- **R4 quality loss by class** reads the quality loss lines, grouped by their `quality_class:` flag. Approved lines take the flag of the candidate they came from. A quality line without a flag shows as "Not classified". Quantities and values come from the loss tree's own rules, so unvalued lines never count as zero. Dispositions are counted by the time the lot was produced; superseded ones are left out.
- **R5 transitions** are measured against the standard the calculation applied (the `transition_standard:` flag on the transition's capacity-ledger lines), never one re-matched in the report. A transition with no lines in a shown run is reported as "not calculated". Excess is duration minus the standard's reference seconds. Each product change shows means over its ended transitions, worst mean excess first. At most 500 transitions per window.
- **R6 reliability trend** sums the reliability report's stored terms per plant month (`YYYY-MM` of the period date), using the same definitions. Assets shown are the 30 with the most eligible failures in the window. "MTBF falling" means MTBF fell in each of the last three months that have a value. This is a plain rule with no percentage threshold.
- **R7 chronic vs sporadic** counts unplanned stops and stops of unknown schedule class (planned and external stops are left out), micro-stops included, from the downtime breakdown cells. Quadrants are cut at the medians of the window's causes that have stops, and a tie counts as high. Chronic means frequent and short, sporadic rare and long, major both high, minor both low. There are no fixed thresholds.
- **R9 coding delay** runs from a stop's end to its first coding with a reason (`event_coding`), shown as the median and 90th percentile in whole seconds and never negative. A stop belongs to the crew and shift of the shift instance holding its start. Coded share and known-state share are the stored KPIs summed per crew or shift label.
- **R10 RCA tracker** uses the app's existing definitions. Open cases are those with any status but "Closed"; overdue actions are open or in progress past their due date, as in the RCA summary. Age buckets are 0–30, 31–60, 61–90 and over 90 days. An action is on time when it was done before the end of its due date's day. The on-time rate counts only actions that have a due date. Claimed and realised savings stay in the cause portfolio.
- **R11 plan adherence** works per product over the plant days of the window, using the plan ledger's target and actual. Volume adherence is Σ min(actual, target) per day / Σ target, so one day's overproduction never makes up for another day's shortfall. Day adherence is the share of planned days on which the grade ran.
- **R12 scorecard** covers the 12 calendar months (`YYYY-MM` of the plant date, as the server groups them) ending with the month of the chosen dates. Utilisation is good / capacity on the capacity ledger, and capacity lost is (capacity − good) / capacity. Plan attainment is actual / target on the plan ledger. Money lost is the plan-ledger loss value in the approved policy's default view, and shows "not fully valued" rather than 0. The budget comparison waits for T1 (targets), which has not been decided.
- **Report exports** (owner 2026-10-20: a designed PDF with the `marun.png` logo; Excel where it fits; `exceljs` approved; provisional exports allowed and stamped). An export pins the report document as read at request time, with the caller's units and money access, on an `ops_report` export job. The worker renders it as a PDF (A4 landscape: logo and title band, provisional stamp, cover facts, KPI tiles, tables whose header repeats, page x of y) or as a workbook (a Summary sheet with the logo, then a sheet per table with real number, percent, `[h]:mm` and date cells). The logo is `assets/marun.png` in the backend, or `REPORT_LOGO_PATH`. Operators can download an export of their own: it holds no money unless the requester may see money, and only an export with money needs the money capability to download. `POST /exports` keeps its formats and types; `xlsx` and `ops_report` are report exports only.
- **Export formats per report:** every report offers PDF and Excel, except the shift handover, which is a document for the next shift and is PDF only. The daily report's export includes the month to date the page shows (`monthFrom`). The monthly statement's export includes the month's certification (`month`).

## 2026-10-21 — Plant settings and branding (owner answers to the settings and branding audit; `plans/SETTINGS_BRANDING_FIXES.md`)

- **Week start is configurable to any day.** `tenants.week_start` accepts the seven weekday names. Monday weeks keep the ISO 8601 key (`2027-W09`); a week starting on any other day is keyed by the date of its first day (`2027-02-24`), in SQL (`weekKeySql`) and TypeScript (`weekKey`) alike. A new plant can choose its week start on the Add plant form; when it doesn't, the 13 Oct rule still applies (Saturday for Asia/Tehran or the Persian calendar, otherwise Monday). Plant settings shows all seven days.
- **Branding fields are applied, not removed.** Secondary colour, favicon and the logo sizing fields take effect.
- **Report exports follow branding.** The PDF/XLSX logo, colours and product name come from the branding settings; `assets/marun.png` is only the fallback when no logo is configured.
- **The product name is "Traverse Forge" everywhere** a user reads it (screens, exports, browser tab, sign-in page). The MQTT message schema titles stay as agreed with data engineering.
- **Engineering defaults taken while fixing the audit findings** (contract changes flagged, OpenAPI snapshot updated):
  - v1 `PUT /api/tenants/:id` refuses `timezone`, `calendarSystem` and `digits` (422 `PLANT_SETTING_V2_ONLY`); v1 app-settings routes refuse the `branding` key (422 `SETTING_V2_ONLY`). The frontend used neither.
  - `X-Client-Audited` is honoured only on routes marked `@ClientAudited()` (none today); everywhere else the server always audits.
  - Plant settings, plant edits and branding are audited by the service that changes them, with `{field: {from, to}}` in the details and a plain sentence ("Changed plant settings of Marun HDPE: week start monday → saturday"), filed under the edited plant. Branding is filed under the plant the administrator acts in (the audit log is plant-scoped).
  - A plant change publishes `PlantSettingsChanged` (every open screen of the plant refetches, the plant directory too); a branding change publishes `BrandingChanged` to every plant. The stored plant selection refreshes its names from the directory.
  - The time zone also locks once a shift calendar is published or shifts were generated.
  - An RCA action or case is overdue once its due plant day is over (the stored due date is the start of that day); the R10 on-time rule uses the same end of day, not the UTC day.
  - Report exports show one reporting currency (the chosen one; rows of other currencies are left out, "Not reported in X" when none).
  - Month buckets follow the plant calendar: a Persian-calendar plant groups by Solar Hijri months (`jalali_month_key`, keys like `1405-07`), in R6, R8, R12, the KPI and loss-value month groupings, the R12 twelve-month window and the PDF month labels.
  - Audit CSV gains "Time (plant)"; export downloads are named `<product>-<plant>-<report>-<plant day>.<ext>`.
  - Branding: the subtitle and initials can be saved empty; stored values are re-validated on read; `GET /v2/branding/public` (no sign-in, cached 5 min) feeds the sign-in page and browser icon; the primary colour becomes the theme's primary (with a readable text colour) and the secondary colour the header accent, only once branding is stored; logo sizes are applied (the header frame never exceeds 48 px). Report exports take the stored name and colours, and the logo when it is an inline PNG or JPEG (the worker never fetches a logo from the web); with no stored branding they keep the original look.

## 2026-10-21 — Setup section in the side navigation (owner: "go with proposed setup section"; `plans/SETUP_NAVIGATION.md`)

- A **Setup** section (between Reliability and Integration) lists everything that sets a plant up, in the order of the setup guide's phases: Setup guide · Plant & equipment (units, assets, products, hierarchy, plant flow) · Codes & failures (codes & rules, failure dictionary) · Production rules (stop and plan policy, standby equipment, unit impact rules, unit state) · Calendars & crews (`/setup/calendars`) · Profiles & rates (`/setup/profiles`: profiles & rates, grade transitions, streams) · RCA library (templates, cause library, verification checklists) · Valuation (`/finance`).
- Defaults on the open points: **Plans** stay under Production (`/planning`, plans only) because they are made every period; **Valuation** moves into Setup; **plant settings** stay in the Admin panel; **Work-order operations** moves to Reliability. The Master data and Finance entries leave Administration.
- Each entry is listed for whoever may open one of its pages (new capabilities `canReachPlantEquipment`, `canReachCodesSetup`, `canReachProductionRules`, `canReachWorkOrders`); route gates do not change — every `/master-data/*` route keeps `canReachMasterData`.
- Every old address still works: `/planning?tab=profiles|transitions|streams|calendars` redirects to the Setup pages (other query parameters kept); record pages keep their `/planning/…` and `/master-data/…` paths. A master-data page's link row shows the pages of its own Setup entry only.

## 2026-10-22 — Setup guide, sidebar and Setup UX (owner answers to the Setup UX audit; `plans/SETUP_UX_FIXES.md`)

- **Setup guide is a reference page only.** `/equipment-setup` lists the six phases and their steps, each with a "Go to <page>" link; no guided session and no setup bar above other pages. An optional unit choice lives in `?unitId=`.
- **Sidebar** sections are an accordion: the section of the current page can be collapsed, and moving to a page in another section opens it and closes the others. Order: Overview (Dashboard, Reports, Pending approvals) · Production · PLA · Downtime analysis · Reliability · Setup · Integration · Administration.
- **Organizations & plants move into Setup** (first entry after the guide); the Admin panel keeps branding and plant settings. After a plant is created the plant directory refreshes and the user is offered to switch to it.
- **Organization administrators reach every plant of their organization** on the server too (`maySelectTenant`), matching the browser; creating a plant grants no role (approval rights stay with roles granted in User management). Creating a plant also creates its default site.
- **Plant on create forms:** unit, asset and product create forms show an Organization › Plant dropdown defaulting to the header plant; changing it switches the selected plant after a confirmation (the server keeps taking the plant from the request context).
- **Site level is exposed:** a Site selector on the unit form and a way to manage a plant's sites (API change, flagged; OpenAPI snapshot updated).
- **Save feedback:** creates, new versions and imports end in a result dialog (View · Add another · Back to list · Next: <next tab>); small edits and approvals use a toast; failures always show a red alert with the offending fields marked, input kept.
- **Next links** appear only in that result dialog and through the area tab bar, which stays visible on sub-pages; no bar on top of pages.

## 2026-10-24 — Dashboard and shell redesign (owner answers; `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-24-dashboard-shell-redesign-design.md`)

- **Sidebar sections renamed:** Production → **Daily operations**, Reliability → **Asset reliability**, Setup → **Configuration**. Section keys and routes are unchanged.
- **USD/IRR display switch is hidden for now;** amounts show in USD.
- **The dashboard follows the header hierarchy selection** (organisation, plant, unit, asset) and every KPI follows it. Selecting an organisation only keeps the acting plant; cross-plant figures are not available yet.
- **KPI tiles show no change or previous-period line.**
- **Plant-level MTBF and MTTR** over the visible units/assets: MTBF = total run time ÷ number of failures; MTTR = total repair time ÷ number of repairs. Awaiting confirmation from the reliability engineers.
- **Explained loss = shortfall − unexplained loss, floored at 0.** Implemented by the server (Task 5).
- Owner removed the dashboard's As-of time and Provisional/Approved badge and the For you row; pending approvals moved to a header icon.
- **`GET /v2/units/asset-presence` is unpaginated by design:** it answers `{ data: { items } }` with one row per visible unit of the plant (bounded by the plant's units), so the hierarchy chevrons need one request.

## 2026-10-24 — Approved and provisional figures are never mixed unlabelled (owner)

- **Dashboard shows approved revisions only:** no draft figures, so no Provisional label is needed there.
- **Reports, Revision = Provisional:** every report whose figures can include draft revisions and has no per-row flag shows one Provisional marker beside its card title; reports whose data does not come from period revisions (RCA actions, lot dispositions, restatements, event status) get none.

## 2026-10-25 — Default revision per page (owner)

- **Downtime and PLA open on Provisional:** the working pages for operators and engineers show the latest figures, drafts included, with the Provisional marker.
- **Reports and Dashboard open on Approved only:** the management pages show signed-off revisions; unapproved periods are left out and the "Incomplete data" note says so.
- The Revision control on each page still switches the view; the split is intentional, so a gap between the PLA and Dashboard totals for one day means unapproved shifts, not an error.

## 2026-10-25 — Remaining open items settled by standards (owner: "do according to standards and common practice")

- **Explained loss (replaces the 2026-10-24 "shortfall − unexplained, floored at 0"):** loss-accounting closure per slice. A slice's unexplained loss is capped at its own shortfall and explained = shortfall − unexplained, both summed over slices, so Explained + Unexplained = Shortfall in every bucket and total and the unexplained share never exceeds 100 %. A slice that met its plan adds no unexplained loss. Values change, the response shape does not.
- **MTBF / MTTR confirmed:** SMRP 3.5.1 / 3.5.2 and ISO 14224 practice. MTBF = total operating time ÷ number of failures, MTTR = total repair time ÷ number of repairs, pooled over the units in view (never an average of unit averages). Time without state data stays out of operating time.
- **PLA waterfall ⓘ texts reworded** in plain terms (no "ledger" or "calculated periods" jargon): Plan target, Sustained capacity, Actual good output (off-grade is a quality loss, not good output).
- **No row counts on keyset-paged lists:** the server has no total, so the pager shows the range instead of a misleading page count (common practice for cursor paging).
- **Refresh rates kept:** dashboard figures once a minute; current stops every 30 s; approval inbox and equipment readiness every 60 s; Event Hub gets new stops by realtime push and re-resolves its rolling window every 5 min (a faster re-resolve would reset paging). Polling stops while the tab is hidden. These suit an analysis/accounting screen; they are not control-room HMI rates.

## 2026-10-26 — MQTT feeds: PLA and downtime topics on one connection (owner)

- **One MQTT connection carries both topics.** Each topic is a feed with a Purpose dropdown (PLA / Downtime), a topic, and a field mapping. The mapping starts from a template and is edited once OT confirms the payloads. This is the new default "PLA and downtime feeds" profile; the contract-v1 profiles stay under "Advanced". Contract: `plans/MQTT_FEEDS.md`; payloads for OT: `docs/MQTT_FEEDS_PAYLOADS.md`.
- **PLA:** OT's actual is stored as an external daily production report, source system `MQTT`, booked to the unit and product chosen on the feed. Target and gap stay Traverse Forge's approved plan. OT's planned and shortfall are kept as `source_figures` and shown next to it ("PLA reports" tab); they are never used in accounting.
- **Downtime:** a stop is placed by an MQTT source mapping, else by unit code, else by asset code (case-insensitive). It then goes through the existing event path: create / revise, reason code, coding rules. A start message opens the stop and a later message with the same `event_id` closes it; a single closed message, or start + duration, also works.
- **Open:** whether a PLA daily report becomes a period actual is left as it is today (it needs a released source status and daily periods). The owner will decide shifts and the rest later.

## 2026-10-26 — First production installation, TF-PREP-001 (owner answers to `deploy/ANALYSIS-REPORT.md`)

- **Reference seed = the HDPE data in the development database** (tenant `HDPE-TRAIN`, organisation `MPC-TRAIN`), extracted by script, never hand-copied. Out of scope: the empty `ASPC`/`HDPE1` branch and the tenant-less product `ple1`.
- **Migrations:** TypeORM stays the authoring tool. Production gets psql-applied, checksummed SQL files (`db/migrations/V001__baseline.sql` = the 140 migrations dumped and moved to schema `forge`, then one `Vnnn` file per later migration, exported by script). Images run with `DB_MIGRATIONS_RUN=false`; boot-time migration is now opt-in (`=true`, as dev `.env` and docker-compose.yml set it).
- **No partition-drop function for now:** `RAW_RETENTION_DAYS` / `SAMPLE_RETENTION_DAYS` stay unset in production (data kept). Revisit before the partitions grow large.
- **One existing login on `instrumental-postgres`** is used for migrations and runtime (`DB_USER`); no separate `traverse_forge_owner` / `traverse_forge_app` roles. The RLS role keeps its name `proact_rls_user` and is granted to that login.
- **No forced password change and no new login throttle** (on-premises deployment). Seeded accounts still get random passwords written to a mode-600 file (superseded 2026-10-28: the 14 HDPE-TRAIN accounts keep their dev password hashes; `30-seed-users.sh` still writes random passwords for additional accounts).
- **Settled by the report's recommendations (owner did not object):** dev directory `server/pro-act-backend` and dev compose names stay; only the runtime names change. `DB_USER` wins over `DB_USERNAME`; `DB_SCHEMA` (production `forge`) sets the search_path. `DB_SSL=true` verifies the server certificate. `FORGE_PLANT_TIMEZONE` is a seed parameter only. The worker becomes a third production service with `uploads` and `exports` volumes. Swagger is off in production (`FORGE_SWAGGER`). `GET /api/health` gains `version` and `commit`, and there is a new public `GET /api/health/ready`. FK indexes are added in an additive `V002`. Backend base image stays Debian bookworm-slim. nginx `PI_SERVER` loses its hard-coded default.

## 2026-10-27 — Independent, daily, OT-declared production loss accounting ("declared mode") (owner approved `docs/PLA_DECLARED_MODE_PLAN.md`)

- **PLA becomes independent of downtime for a plant that opts in.** A new accounting policy kind `loss_accounting` (versioned, effective-dated, approved) carries `grain` (shift / day), `dayStart`, `lossBasis` (computed / declared), `targetSource` (plan / declared), `accountingUnitId`, `accountingProductId` ("Accounting product"), `stopsCrossCheck`, `identityTolerance`. Every existing plant keeps `shift` + `computed`; HDPE switches to `day` + `declared`.
- **D1 Target = OT's planned figure** (`targetSource = declared`); the Traverse Forge plan stays available as the alternative.
- **D2 Identity:** `planned − actual ≠ shortfall` beyond the tolerance stores the event, raises a review item and blocks submission until OT revises or an engineer books the difference as a tolerance line. Only a message with no shortfall at all is quarantined.
- **D3 One production loss event per unit per day;** a second `event_id` for the same window raises a review item; never summed.
- **D4 One slice per period in declared mode;** product campaigns do not split the declared figure.
- **D5 Certify day stays** as a separate step even with one period per day.
- **D6 Loss line kind = the reason code's OEE component;** a reason without a component books as `unaccounted_classified`.
- **D7 Manual production loss events** are allowed for event writers; a later OT event for the same window supersedes after confirmation.
- **D8 OT status:** `DETECTED` and `CONFIRMED` both create the event; a day is approved only when the status is `CONFIRMED`.
- **D9 The PLA feed no longer writes an external daily report;** the production loss event is the record and the "PLA reports" tab becomes a link to the Event Hub.
- **D10 HDPE accounting unit = Extruder1** for the pilot; stops stay on their equipment.
- **D11 `lost_production_t` is dropped from the downtime template** (never used).
- **D12 Effective date:** the new regime applies from the policy's effective date; approved periods before it are untouched, open ones are re-materialized.
- **Standards note (owner asked):** this is top-down loss accounting (production balance plan − actual, coded by reason, ISO 22400 / Meridium PLA practice). Downtime stays the supporting evidence (cross-check figure, reliability KPIs) rather than the pricing engine; OEE and capacity-gap views are not available in declared mode.
- **Build-time rulings (27 Oct 2026).** (a) Days already calculated under shifts stay on shift accounting when a plant switches to day grain; the effective date is the approval date (no future scheduling). (b) When the OT actual is missing, the measured good production is used and the period is flagged `declared_actual_missing`. (c) **Accounting product** (`accountingProductId`): the product the declared slice is booked to when the accounting unit runs several products and no plan covers the day; the slice's source is labelled `profile_default`. (d) **Accounting unit only:** the declared basis and the daily grain apply to the accounting unit (`accountingUnitId`, null = the version's unit or every unit); the other units stay computed per shift, and the settings form drafts a declared version for that unit. Period pages show each period's own run basis.
- **Final review fixes (27 Oct 2026).** (e) A duplicate declaration refuses submission (`DECLARED_DUPLICATE`), raises `duplicate_declared_loss` on both events, and the engine's pick is deterministic (earliest start, earliest `created_at`, lowest id; `declared_loss_chosen:<id>`). (f) A plant already on daily accounting cannot change grain, day start or accounting unit yet (422 `GRAIN_CHANGE_UNSUPPORTED`; follow-up). (g) A negative declared shortfall is refused. (h) Day target with `targetSource = plan` = the pro-rated sum of every plan period the day overlaps. **Follow-up, not built:** D7 "an OT event supersedes a manual event after confirmation".
- **Task 13 statement URL contract (as implemented, 27 Oct 2026).** The Accounting statement page is opened as `/pla-analytics/statement?from=<period start>&to=<period end>&window=dates&units=<unit id>&focus=<period id>&source=event:<event id>` (`source` only when opened from an event; it adds the back link). The task brief's `unitIds` / `window=custom` / `from=event:` wording was illustrative: the PLA section's existing parameter names (`units`, `window=dates`, `from` / `to` = the period's UTC start and end) win, and `from` stays the window start. The API keeps `unitIds` (`GET /v2/reports/loss-statement`).

## 2026-10-28 — TF-PREP-001 Phases 3–8 rulings

Evidence: `deploy/PREP-LOG.md` Phases 3–8; procedure: `deploy/vm/VM-STEPS.template.md`.

- **Seeded accounts keep their development password hashes** (owner instruction: "these users"; no forced change, decision 2026-10-26). The 14 `@hdpe-train.mpc` accounts arrive through the git-ignored `db/seed/generated/U010__users.sql`; further accounts come from `deploy/db/30-seed-users.sh` with random passwords. Because it carries those hashes, the release bundle is sensitive and is handled like a backup.
- **V001 = the 144 TypeORM migrations** (through `1794241400000`) dumped and rewritten to schema `forge`; **V002 = the FK indexes migration**, exported through the per-release logging path (`export-migration.sh`), so that path is proven before production depends on it.
- **Extensions stay in `public`** (`uuid-ossp`, `btree_gist`; `pg_stat_statements` optional and skipped when not preloaded or not permitted). The application search_path is `forge, public`.
- **Migration ledger `forge.deploy_migrations`** (version, file, SHA-256 checksum, applied_at, applied_by). `10-migrate.sh` refuses a file whose checksum differs from the ledger before applying anything; each file runs in one transaction with its ledger row and the RLS grants.
- **Default privileges are set by `00-create-db-and-roles.sh` for `DB_USER`** in `DB_SCHEMA` (tables and sequences to `proact_rls_user`); the dev dump's `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE postgres` lines are dropped from V001 so the dev login name never reaches production SQL.
- **Integration connections are seeded inactive and without secrets** (`is_active = false`, `secret_ref` NULL), together with their signals, so state and source mappings resolve; credentials are entered on the VM in Settings.
- **`/piwebapi` is opt-in:** the production nginx proxies it only when `PI_SERVER` is set; empty answers 404.
- **Dev compose names are unchanged** (`proact-*`, `docker-compose.yml`); the production names live only in `deploy/docker-compose.forge.yml` (project `traverse-forge`).
- **`rollback.sh` never reverts migrations:** V files are additive and the previous release runs on the newer schema; a non-additive release ships its own rollback plan, restoring a backup only under that plan.
- **Backups are private:** `backup-forge.sh` writes mode 600 files in a mode 700 folder (newest `BACKUP_KEEP`, default 14) and archives uploads with the running frontend image (no extra image on the air-gapped VM).
- **A superuser `DB_USER` bypasses RLS outside requests, as in dev** (requests still `SET ROLE proact_rls_user`). A non-superuser `DB_USER` needs CREATEDB + CREATEROLE (or a DBA pre-creates the database, `proact_rls_user` and the grant) plus two superuser-only grants: `GRANT SET ON PARAMETER app.tenant_ids` (V001's functions set it) and `BYPASSRLS` (pg_dump of forced-RLS tables in `backup-forge.sh`); `00-create-db-and-roles.sh` checks them.
- **The generated passwords file never sits inside a git work tree:** `30-seed-users.sh` and the CLI refuse such a path; the default is `$FORGE_HOME/private` (mode 700).
